ONE frozen batch, at most SIX charged mathematical child jobs, TEN external hard-timeout seconds each, total SIXTY accounted seconds. At most one complete graph-feasibility analysis per case. Mathematical interface, to derive briefly as text BEFORE freezing code: given one fixed independent-vertex family v_i and actual cumulative physical displacement P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k, the unknown initial inner products F_i=<z0,v_i> must satisfy
 F_j-F_i <= C_ij, C_ij=<P_i+v_i,v_j-v_i>, for EVERY original i,j.
The nonzero vertices are independent in the full Walsh dictionary, so any real F vector has a unique actual z0. Simultaneous nonzero-vertex normals are equivalent to these difference constraints. Adding the SAME scalar to every F_i preserves them and can pay every zero-vertex lower bound F_i>=<P_i+v_i,v_i>. Thus a negative directed cycle in the ORIGINAL C array rules out EVERY initial point and EVERY relative value choice for this fixed gradient itinerary, independently of constant-blend restrictions. Conversely a numerical potential with small residual is only a numerical candidate, not a proof. A general graph alternative may be proved from path sums, but no full-depth sign conclusion follows from finite data.

The SIX cases are fixed in this order:
1. Feasible directed-constraint fixture C=[[0,2,3],[0,0,-1],[5,3,0]], whose explicit potential F=(0,1,-1) is feasible.
2. The same fixture with C01=-3 instead of2. The original two-edge cycle0->1->0 has total-3. This must be detected with orientation correct. These two fixtures are graph checks, not GD certificates.
3-6. Physical/dictionary depths j=6,8,9,10, always using ONE enclosing M=1536 for the word parameters eps=1/(4M), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c. Set N=2^j, n=N-1, actual h_i=1+a*c^nu2(i+1), i<n. The dictionary has j bits and N vertices. This is a FINITE ANALOGUE: changing dictionary N changes its radial damping and tiny-character weights; it is NOT the restriction of the full N=2^1536 geometry, NOT physical depth>=1536, and NOT a theorem under the old full-depth hypotheses. Preserve this limitation in every report.

For each production case use the exact intended frozen formula:
 R=sqrt3, v=1/3, Kmix=N-1-j, eta=1/(N^2*Kmix), T=4/3+1/N^2;
 E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta*(N/2-l-1), G_l=T-2E_l;
 rho_l is the positive root of r^2+(H_l-2)*(G_l/T)*r+1-H_l=0, H_l=1+a*c^l;
 r0=rho0*(1-4/N), r1=rho1*(1-4/N), r_l=1 for l>=2;
 sigma0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i*r_(nu2(i+1));
 Gram_ij=sigma_i*sigma_j*[1/3-eta+sum_(k<j)(2^k/(N-1)-eta)*(-1)^(bit_k(i)+bit_k(j))+eta*N*1_(i=j)].
In this last formula the summation dimension is the CASE depth; use distinct code variable names so the second vertex index is not confused with depth j. Retain all tiny mixed-character terms. The chronological prefix inner-product matrix is A_ij=sum_(k<i)h_k Gram_kj. Then C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii. Save the full original arrays, not a reduced adjacent or symmetric system. # Frozen interface and numerical method

Allocation ddd2a22025cdbaac; model/effort supplied as gpt-6-astra / ultra.
This text is written before any numerical import or mathematical evaluation.
All mathematical conclusions remain unverified; this is numerical discovery.

Write P_i=sum_(k<i) h_k v_k and F_i=<z0,v_i>. The normal at z0-P_i
against v_j is <z0-P_i-v_i,v_j-v_i><=0. Expanding gives exactly
F_j-F_i<=<P_i+v_i,v_j-v_i>=C_ij. With Gram_ij=<v_i,v_j> and
A_ij=sum_(k<i)h_k Gram_kj, this is C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii.
All original ordered pairs are required, including the terminal vertex.
There is no physical step h_n. For the zero vertex the normal is
-F_i+<P_i+v_i,v_i><=0, so a common shift of all F_i pays these finite
lower bounds. The full positive Walsh dictionary and positive radii make
the nonzero vertices independent, so every real vector F is represented by
one actual z0. Thus an original negative cycle excludes every initializer
for this fixed itinerary, not just a constant-blend value law.

Summing F_next-F_current<=C_current,next around a directed cycle gives
0<=sum C_current,next. Conversely, if there is no negative cycle, append
a zero-weight source to every vertex. Every walk can have its nonnegative
cycles deleted; finitely many simple paths attain shortest distances.
Appending any edge to a shortest path proves d_j<=d_i+C_ij. This is a
general exact finite-graph alternative, not a full-depth sign theorem.

The child uses ONE synchronous Bellman-Ford/min-plus run. Starting with
d_i=0, in each round it finds min_i(d_i+C_ij), then retains d_j if no
strict raw floating-point improvement occurs. At most N rounds run.
Each strict improvement records the incoming original edge. No tolerance
is inserted into relaxation, no edge is dropped, and C is never repaired.
If a round has no changes, the full original residual F_j-F_i-C_ij is
reported. After N changing rounds, ONE deterministic predecessor chain
from the smallest changed vertex is followed N times; its directed cycle
is reconstructed, and the original raw weights are summed in the child.
Numerical predecessor anomalies or a nonnegative reconstructed sum remain
unresolved defects rather than triggering a second extraction or retry.

Frozen reporting tolerance: machine epsilon e for binary64; S=max(1,max|C|);
t_edge=256*e*N*S; t_cycle=cycle_edge_count*t_edge. These are reporting
thresholds, not proved roundoff bounds. A cycle with fsum(weights)<-t_cycle
is robustly negative numerically; a raw negative sum within the threshold
is unresolved. A no-cycle-detected candidate requires the maximum ORIGINAL
residual<=t_edge. It is not an exact potential or a legal GD lower witness.
Raw fsum, ordinary sum, numpy sum, individual edge weights and residuals
are retained even when they disagree with a qualitative classification.

The six frozen jobs are the two prescribed fixtures and depths 6,8,9,10.
All four depths use enclosing word parameter M=1536, but dictionary N=2^depth
changes the radial damping and mixed-character weights. They are FINITE
ANALOGUES, not restrictions of N=2^1536 geometry and not depth>=1536.
There is no payoff, radius, blend, stepsize, SDP or benchmark optimization.

The stdlib broker charges all ten seconds and one graph-analysis slot before
launching each child through the existing public mathematical launcher.
Each child runs in a new process group, with all imports, construction,
relaxation, cycle extraction, array saves and numerical diagnostics inside
an externally enforced ten-second deadline. No numerical code runs in the
broker. Calibration failure closes the allocation and retires remaining
credit; every failure counts. Source and case hashes are frozen first.
After close, only saved-text transcription, administrative reads and byte
hashing are allowed. No mathematical extraction or automatic renewal.

 R=sqrt3, v=1/3, Kmix=N-1-j, eta=1/(N^2*Kmix), T=4/3+1/N^2;
 E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta*(N/2-l-1), G_l=T-2E_l;
 rho_l is the positive root of r^2+(H_l-2)*(G_l/T)*r+1-H_l=0, H_l=1+a*c^l;
 r0=rho0*(1-4/N), r1=rho1*(1-4/N), r_l=1 for l>=2;
 sigma0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i*r_(nu2(i+1));
 Gram_ij=sigma_i*sigma_j*[1/3-eta+sum_(k<j)(2^k/(N-1)-eta)*(-1)^(bit_k(i)+bit_k(j))+eta*N*1_(i=j)].
In this last formula the summation dimension is the CASE depth; use distinct code variable names so the second vertex index is not confused with depth j. Retain all tiny mixed-character terms. The chronological prefix inner-product matrix is A_ij=sum_(k<i)h_k Gram_kj. Then C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii. Save the full original arrays, not a reduced adjacent or symmetric system. The terminal vertex n is retained; there is no physical h_n.

Use a transparent Bellman-Ford/min-plus difference-constraint analysis with an explicit maximum of N relaxation rounds. At most ONE such feasibility run per production case. A detected cycle must be reconstructed in original index order, and its ACTUAL directed edges, raw weights and raw summed weight must be saved inside that SAME child job. Preserve numerical residuals and all floating defects. For a numerical feasible outcome save the complete potential and max original inequality residual; do not call it exact or a legal lower witness. No payoff optimization, initial-radius optimization, alpha/lambda search, additional parameter choice, SDP or benchmark DP.

Choose and freeze a scale-aware reporting tolerance before the batch; retain raw signed cycle values separately. A robust negative cycle, a tiny unresolved negative quantity, no detected cycle and a timeout are distinct outcomes. Do not repair Gram eigenvalues, average nonsymmetric data, clip signs, alter C, change steps/roots or retry a failing case. No post-close mathematical extraction is allowed. Administrative copying/transcription and byte hashing of saved output is allowed after close.

Execution discipline: write child.py, broker.py, CASES.json and a concise derivation note in THIS directory. The stdlib-only broker must charge each job's full ten accounted seconds BEFORE launching its child. Freeze source/case hashes BEFORE any numerical library import or mathematical evaluation. Each child imports the numerical runtime, constructs every array, performs its one graph analysis, extracts/sums any cycle, computes all diagnostics and writes all mathematical output under a TEN-second external process-group hard timeout. # Handoff: original Gray count repaired, same restricted constant

**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** Allocation 01232786b036b8ec; resume record
412153cf9153438d; one native gpt-6-astra / ultra analytical invocation,
2026-10-08. Original minimax target OPEN. No accepted fact was created.

## Result available for review

REPORT.md supplies a self-contained corrected proof attempt that, for every
integer m>=1536, all initializers realizing the prescribed original
damping-four Gray vertex itinerary obey

    2 Phi(z_n) / dist(z_0,argmin Phi)^2
       < 343/(333 D_m) < 17/(16 D_m),
    D_m=1+2 sum_(k<n) h_k.

Its zero-adjoined supremum obeys the same strict bound. Legal-initializer
existence is not asserted. This is not an upper bound on every trajectory
of this body or on the full function class, and it is not an answer to
the original minimax problem.

The immutable governing reports were read completely and hashed:

- 81cec34e1a158b2b.md:
  c689b2d90a6b981e180522e195cfa64f32d795ca6850ffb3c8bdcc24c3ed2af3.
- a37b81ea81b53910.md:
  abd330128e7f27d981712b19fca4fa0026ce2b87e91cb10b78b3ee2d49366356.
- New TASK.md:
  4687d9c90651ef0697451f10927d8b9c067f80229893739244a7d96b23ecb8b1.

## Exact repair

The physical word remains h_k=1+a c^nu2(k+1), with N=2^m,n=N-1,
epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+epsilon), a=beta/c.
The original ratios remain

    x=rho_0(1-4/N), y=rho_1(1-4/N), r_l=1 for l>=2.

At vertex k, the completed level-one step labels are 2,6,10,...<=k.
Therefore

    sigma_k=x^ceil(k/2) y^floor((k+2)/4).

Only k=4q+2 differs from the defective closed formula: its actual radius
is xy theta^q, with theta=x^2 y. The polynomial's low coefficient becomes
xy H_0 z^3 w, and the scalar U_j numerator becomes
H_0(1+xy)+xH_1. REPORT 5 records exact changes in U_j,M_j,Z_j, every
pair prefix, both ordered pair constraints, both zero constraints, the
terminal constant, and the certificate denominator. Full matrix changes
from the defective expression are also given.

The actual inherited pair stays i=N/4-1,n=N-1. Its bridge radii
s=theta^(N/16),t=theta^(N/4)=s^4 and pair Gram are unchanged.
All tiny Walsh characters, the diagonal reverse-row correction, and the
terminal t^2 T/2 remain present.

## Why the constant survives

The repaired residue-two ratio is

    sigma_k / exp(-zeta(k+1)/N) = x^(-1/2)y^(1/4),
    zeta=-(N/4)log(theta).

For the unchanged damping-four roots, the report derives x<y<1.
Consequently all four ratio logarithms remain nonnegative and bounded by
the same envelope, giving

    0 <= sigma_k-exp(-zeta(k+1)/N) < 8/N.

The independent damping-four estimates are

    33/10<zeta<10/3, 3/7<s<4/9, t<1/27,
    0<T+B<7/6, D_m<12S, S=S_(m-2).

The corrected physical prefix scalar is

    J=J_i+J_pre+(1+s^3)(J_n-J_pre).

Keeping all signed quarter contributions and original bridges gives
J_ideal/S>7/15+38/7203; the corrected envelope gives
|J-J_ideal|<256S/N, hence J>7S/15. This controls the same single
positive multiplier ell=2t/(s+t) on the original i->n constraint:

    kappa=ell s[J+sT-tB+(s+t)tT/(2s)] > ell s J.

The exact weak-dual square bound is then strictly below
t(T+B)/[(1+s^3)J], producing the original 343/(333D_m).
The reverse pair and zero constraints remain in the relaxation; their
multipliers are zero. No claim that the sign of each prefix correction is
favorable is used.

## Source-only numerical finding

The corrected-cycle public child source, lines 57-64, obtains the actual
step valuations and constructs sigma by cumulative products. Lines 72-75
construct Gram,A,C from those radii. The explicitly referenced predecessor
uses the same lines. Its former correction at line 182 concerns JSON
bool typing only.

The later common-shift source, lines 45-65, loads the original saved
Gram,A,C,sigma,h and archived potential. Its production CASES.json points
to the corrected-cycle depth-6,8,9,10 packets. It does not rebuild Gray
radii from a closed count. Thus this specific analytical count error does
not appear in the inspected construction code.

No archive was loaded, and no floating score, residual, feasibility
claim, or graph outcome was certified. Full source hashes and exact line
references are in REPORT 9 and CORRECTION_LEDGER.md.

## Evidence and continuation boundary

REPORT.md is the full derivation; CORRECTION_LEDGER.md maps each affected
old equation and source-code location. Both the old erroneous source and
the later damping-two source remain unchanged. The later report's
changed-damping estimates were not imported.

If a future allocation reviews this result, its exact claim is the
restricted theorem above, and the central repaired step is REPORT (6.8).
A future review would still need to assess this entire joint draft before
any acceptance. Nothing in this allocation establishes full itinerary
feasibility, a full-function upper certificate, or the original
all-schedule lower bound.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002481 -->
# Corrected level-one count for the original damping-four Gray itinerary

**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** One independent analytical invocation, allocated on
2026-10-08 under allocation 01232786b036b8ec and resumption
412153cf9153438d. This report contains a complete corrected proof attempt
for the restricted statement below. It is not a verifier-accepted fact.
The original minimax objective remains OPEN.

## 1. Result and audit boundary

For the actual chronological damping-four geometry defined in Section 2,
the proposed uniform conclusion survives:

\[
 \sup\left(\{0\}\cup
 \left\{\frac{2\Phi(z_n)}
 {\operatorname{dist}(z_0,\arg\min\Phi)^2}:
 z_0\text{ realizes the prescribed full vertex itinerary}\right\}\right)
 <\frac{343}{333D_m}<\frac{17}{16D_m},
 \qquad m\ge1536,
\tag{1.1}
\]

where \(D_m=1+2\sum_{k<n}h_k\). This is a bound on initializers
realizing one prescribed itinerary of one fixed body. Existence of such
an initializer is not asserted. Arbitrary trajectories on the body,
the full worst-case function class, and the original minimax limit are
outside this conclusion. The factor two uses the original normalization
\(\mathcal W=2\mathcal R\).

The error in the old report is substantive: its closed count, polynomial,
and derived finite prefix expressions do not represent its stated
chronological law. The correction is made here without changing that law.
The final constant survives because the corrected radii satisfy the same
uniform envelope needed by the signed prefix proof, not because smaller
radii would automatically improve the score.

Both complete public reports were read, and their byte hashes matched:

| Source | SHA-256 |
|---|---|
| shared_evidence/81cec34e1a158b2b.md, damping four | c689b2d90a6b981e180522e195cfa64f32d795ca6850ffb3c8bdcc24c3ed2af3 |
| shared_evidence/a37b81ea81b53910.md, damping two | abd330128e7f27d981712b19fca4fa0026ce2b87e91cb10b78b3ee2d49366356 |
| This allocation's TASK.md | 4687d9c90651ef0697451f10927d8b9c067f80229893739244a7d96b23ecb8b1 |

The second report identifies the correct count; none of its damping-two
radial windows, prefix inequalities, feasible points, or score bounds is
used below. Verification depth is source-bound with independent analytical
derivation. No mathematical code or saved mathematical array was executed
or loaded.

## 2. Frozen geometry and the chronological count

Fix a single integer \(m\ge1536\), used in the entire word and every prefix:

\[
 N=2^m,\quad n=N-1,\quad \epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad
 \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\frac\beta c,
\]
\[
 H_\ell=1+ac^\ell,\qquad h_k=H_{\nu_2(k+1)},\quad 0\le k<n.
\tag{2.1}
\]

Here \(H_\ell\) is a single step at level \(\ell\), not a complete word.
All steps are positive. For integers \(0\le j\le m\), the depth-\(j\) physical sum is

\[
 S_j=\sum_{k<2^j-1}h_k
 =\frac{ac^j}{c-2}+
   \left(1-\frac a{c-2}\right)2^j-1.
\tag{2.2}
\]

Indeed there are \(2^{j-\ell-1}\) occurrences of level \(\ell<j\);
summing the constant and geometric terms gives (2.2).

Set \(K_{\rm mix}=N-1-m\) and \(\eta=1/(N^2K_{\rm mix})\).
For the nonzero binary characters \(b\in\mathbb F_2^m\), let
\(\chi_b(u)=(-1)^{b\cdot u}\). The distinguished characters are
\(b^{(j)}=N-2^j\), \(0\le j<m\). Their squared amplitudes are
\(2^j/(N-1)\); every other nonzero character has squared amplitude
\(\eta\). Retain them all. Define

\[
 w_k=((\omega_b\chi_b(\operatorname{Gray}(k)))_{b\ne0},1/\sqrt3),
 \qquad \operatorname{Gray}(k)=k\mathbin{\rm xor}\lfloor k/2\rfloor,
 \qquad T=\frac43+\frac1{N^2}.
\]

Cancellation of successive Gray bits gives
\(\chi_{b^{(j)}}(\operatorname{Gray}(k))=(-1)^{\operatorname{bit}_j(k)}\).
The sum over all binary characters is \(N\) at the zero label and zero
elsewhere, by pairing characters in a coordinate with nonzero label.
Subtracting the constant and distinguished terms gives the exact kernel

\[
 B_{k\ell}:=\langle w_k,w_\ell\rangle
 =T-\frac{2(k\mathbin{\rm xor}\ell)}{N-1}
    +2\eta\operatorname{pop}(k\mathbin{\rm xor}\ell)
    -\eta N+\eta N\,1_{k=\ell}.
\tag{2.3}
\]

In particular \(B_{kk}=T\). At a level-\(\ell\) adjacent step, the two
indices have xor \(2^{\ell+1}-1\), of popcount \(\ell+1\). Substituting
in (2.3) gives

\[
 E_\ell=\frac{2^{\ell+1}-1}{N-1}
          +\eta(N/2-\ell-1),\qquad B_\ell^{\rm adj}=T-2E_\ell.
\]

Let \(\rho_\ell\) be the positive root of

\[
 \rho_\ell^2+(H_\ell-2)(B_\ell^{\rm adj}/T)\rho_\ell+1-H_\ell=0.
\]

Preserve exactly the original law:

\[
 x=\rho_0(1-4/N),\quad y=\rho_1(1-4/N),\quad
 r_\ell=1\ \ (\ell\ge2),\quad
 \sigma_0=1,\quad
 \sigma_{k+1}=\sigma_k r_{\nu_2(k+1)},\quad r_0=x,\ r_1=y.
\tag{2.4}
\]

Before vertex \(k\), the completed steps have positive integer labels
\(1,\ldots,k\). The level-zero labels are the odd integers, numbering
\(\lceil k/2\rceil\). The level-one labels are \(2,6,10,\ldots\), numbering
\(\lfloor(k+2)/4\rfloor\), also when \(k=0,1\). Thus

\[
 \boxed{\sigma_k=x^{\lceil k/2\rceil}y^{\lfloor(k+2)/4\rfloor}}.
\tag{2.5}
\]

With \(\theta=x^2y\), the four residue classes are

| \(k\) | Actual \(\sigma_k\) |
|---|---|
| \(4q\) | \(\theta^q\) |
| \(4q+1\) | \(x\theta^q\) |
| \(4q+2\) | \(xy\theta^q\) |
| \(4q+3\) | \(\theta^{q+1}\) |

The old count omits the completed level-one step precisely at \(k=4q+2\).
For example its value at \(k=2\) is \(x\), whereas the first two completed
ratios in (2.4) give \(xy\). No chronological step is changed by (2.5).

Put \(v_k=\sigma_kw_k\), \(D_0=\operatorname{conv}\{0,v_0,\ldots,v_n\}\),
and let \(V\) have columns \(v_k\), with \(G=V^TV\). The full Walsh
matrix, with its nonzero constant row, is invertible by the character
orthogonality just proved. Positive row amplitudes and positive column
radii preserve this property. Therefore \(V\) is a basis of
\(\mathbb R^N\) and \(G\) is positive definite.

## 3. Legal initializers, actual objective, and true normalization

Define the original chronological constants

\[
 P_k=\sum_{b<k}h_bv_b,\quad A_{k\ell}=\langle P_k,v_\ell\rangle,\quad
 C_{k\ell}=A_{k\ell}-A_{kk}+G_{k\ell}-G_{kk},
\]
\[
 K_k=A_{kk}+G_{kk},\qquad c_n=A_{nn}+\tfrac12G_{nn}.
\tag{3.1}
\]

The initializer potentials \(p_k=\langle z_0,v_k\rangle\) are not function
values. Every real \(p\) has the unique realization \(z_0=VG^{-1}p\).
For the fixed projection function

\[
 \Phi(z)=\max_{v\in D_0}\{\langle z,v\rangle-\tfrac12\|v\|^2\},
\tag{3.2}
\]

completing the square gives the unique maximizer \(\Pi_{D_0}z\).
A point \(v\in D_0\) is the projection iff
\(\langle z-v,w-v\rangle\le0\) for every generating vertex \(w\), including
zero. Necessity follows from segment derivatives of squared distance;
sufficiency follows by expanding squared distance. Adding two such normal
inequalities gives firm nonexpansiveness, and hence the 1-Lipschitz
property of the projection. The two maximizing affine functions bound
the first-order remainder of (3.2) between zero and
\(\|\Pi_{D_0}(z+h)-\Pi_{D_0}z\|\|h\|\le\|h\|^2\).
Thus \(\Phi\) is finite, convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth, with gradient
\(\Pi_{D_0}\).

At \(z_k=z_0-P_k\), all these projection normals are exactly

\[
 p_\ell-p_k\le C_{k\ell}\quad\text{for all original }k,\ell,\qquad
 p_k\ge K_k\quad\text{for all original }k.
\tag{3.3}
\]

They are necessary and sufficient for the complete prescribed itinerary,
including the terminal vertex. Under (3.3), the GD recursion has precisely
the physical steps \(h_k\), and

\[
 \Phi(z_k)=p_k-A_{kk}-\tfrac12G_{kk},\qquad
 \Phi(z_n)=p_n-c_n\ge\tfrac12G_{nn}>0.
\tag{3.4}
\]

There is no step \(h_n\). The entire minimum set is

\[
 K_{\rm pol}=\{z:V^Tz\le0\},\qquad \min\Phi=0.
\tag{3.5}
\]

At such a polar point all terms in (3.2) are nonpositive and zero is
available. If instead \(\langle z,v_k\rangle>0\), a sufficiently small
positive multiple of \(v_k\) belongs to \(D_0\) and gives a positive value.
This proves the converse.

Changing variables \(d=V^T(z_0-z_\star)\) gives the exact closest-minimum
radius

\[
 R_{\rm true}^2=\operatorname{dist}(z_0,K_{\rm pol})^2
 =r(p):=\min_{d\ge p}d^TG^{-1}d.
\tag{3.6}
\]

The quadratic is coercive, so the minimum exists. Its coordinate
optimality conditions give
\(\alpha=G^{-1}d^*\ge0\) and
\(\alpha_k(d_k^*-p_k)=0\). Consequently \(V\alpha\) belongs to the positive
cone, \(z_\star=z_0-V\alpha\) is polar, and
\(\langle V\alpha,z_\star\rangle=0\). Expansion against any polar point
proves that this is the closest point. In particular \(p_0\ge K_0=G_{00}>0\)
makes every legal initializer's radius positive.

For completeness, the perspective formulation is

\[
 \sup (2F_n-2\tau c_n),\quad
 \tau\ge0,\quad F_\ell-F_k\le\tau C_{k\ell},\quad F_k\ge\tau K_k,
 \quad d\ge F,\quad d^TG^{-1}d\le\tau.
\tag{3.7}
\]

A legal \(p\) gives \(\tau=1/r(p),F=\tau p,d=\tau d^*\), with objective
equal to its actual normalized score. Conversely, a feasible point with
\(\tau>0\) gives a legal \(p=F/\tau\) and \(r(p)\le1/\tau\);
(3.4) makes its score at least the perspective objective. At \(\tau=0\),
positive definiteness forces \(d=0\), and \(0\le F\le d\) forces \(F=0\).
Thus (3.7) equals the zero-adjoined supremum in (1.1). It is not the
conventional supremum of an empty legal set.

This perspective is compact: by Cauchy--Schwarz,
\(d^TG^{-1}d\ge d_0^2/G_{00}\ge\tau^2G_{00}\), so
\(\tau\le1/G_{00}\) when positive. Its quadratic constraint bounds \(d\);
\(\tau K\le F\le d\) then bounds \(F\). It is closed. A positive maximum
therefore has a legal realization; no positive feasibility is assumed.

All statements in this section use the corrected full \(V,G,A,C,K,c_n\).
Their algebraic form is unchanged by the count correction.

## 4. Corrected finite polynomial and complete inherited pair data

Fix exactly

\[
 r=m-2,\quad L_q=2^r=N/4,\quad i=L_q-1,\qquad
 s=\sigma_i=\theta^{L_q/4},\quad
 t=\sigma_n=\theta^{N/4}=s^4.
\tag{4.1}
\]

Both indices are \(3\pmod4\), so their radii were already counted
correctly. Their original kernel and Gram matrix are

\[
 B=B_{in}=T-\frac{3N}{2(N-1)}+4\eta-\eta N,\qquad
 G_{\rm pair}=\begin{pmatrix}s^2T&stB\\stB&t^2T\end{pmatrix}.
\tag{4.2}
\]

The xor is \(3N/4\), of popcount two. The principal Gram matrix is positive
definite. Its kernel satisfies \(B<0\), and

\[
 0<T+B=\frac76+\frac2{N^2}-\frac3{2(N-1)}-\eta(N-4)<\frac76.
\tag{4.3}
\]

The lower bound follows from positive definiteness; the upper bound uses
\(2/N^2<3/[2(N-1)]\). For \(B<0\), its expression is
\(-1/6+1/N^2-3/[2(N-1)]-\eta(N-4)\).

For \(2\le j\le m\), with empty products equal to one, define

\[
\begin{split}
 {\cal P}_j(z,w)={}&
 [H_0z+xH_1z^2w+xyH_0z^3w]
 \prod_{b=0}^{j-3}[1+w(\theta z^4)^{2^b}]\\
 &+\sum_{\ell=2}^{j-1}H_\ell w^\ell(\theta z^4)^{2^{\ell-2}}
 \prod_{b=0}^{j-\ell-2}[1+w(\theta z^4)^{2^{\ell-1+b}}].
\end{split}
\tag{4.4}
\]

It equals \(\sum_{k<2^j-1}h_k\sigma_kz^{k+1}w^{\operatorname{pop}(k)}\).
For \(k=4q,4q+1,4q+2\), use the three radii in Section 2 and popcounts
\(\operatorname{pop}(q),\operatorname{pop}(q)+1,\operatorname{pop}(q)+1\).
For \(\ell\ge2\), write \(k=(2q+1)2^\ell-1\); its radius is
\(\theta^{(2q+1)2^{\ell-2}}\) and popcount is
\(\ell+\operatorname{pop}(q)\). Binary expansion of \(q\) expands the
products in (4.4), covering exactly these disjoint classes.

Put

\[
 U_j={\cal P}_j(1,1),\quad
 M_j=(\partial_z{\cal P}_j)(1,1)-U_j,\quad
 Z_j=(\partial_w{\cal P}_j)(1,1).
\tag{4.5}
\]

They sum respectively \(h_k\sigma_k\), \(kh_k\sigma_k\), and
\(\operatorname{pop}(k)h_k\sigma_k\). Summing the finite geometric series
in each level also gives the corrected scalar expression

\[
 U_j=(1-\theta^{2^{j-2}})
 \left[
 \frac{H_0(1+xy)+xH_1}{1-\theta}
 +\sum_{\ell=2}^{j-1}
 \frac{H_\ell\theta^{2^{\ell-2}}}{1-\theta^{2^{\ell-1}}}
 \right].
\tag{4.6}
\]

All denominators are positive. Write \(U=U_r,M=M_r,Z=Z_r\), and define

\[
\begin{gathered}
 b_i=T-\frac{2(L_q-1)}{N-1}+2\eta r-\eta N,\qquad
 b_n=T-2+2\eta m-\eta N,\\
 J_i=b_iU+\frac{2M}{N-1}-2\eta Z,\qquad
 J_{\rm pre}=b_nU+\frac{2M}{N-1}-2\eta Z,\\
 J_n=b_nU_m+\frac{2M_m}{N-1}-2\eta Z_m.
\end{gathered}
\tag{4.7}
\]

Using \(k\mathbin{\rm xor}i=i-k\) for \(k<i\), and
\(k\mathbin{\rm xor}n=n-k\), these are exactly
\(\langle P_i,w_i\rangle,\langle P_i,w_n\rangle,\langle P_n,w_n\rangle\).

The reverse row also retains every original step. Put \(d_{\rm Q}=2L_q/(N-1)\)
(this symbol is a scalar, not the optimization variable in (3.6)), and

\[
\begin{split}
 E_i={}&\sum_{q=0}^{3}s^q[J_i+(-qd_{\rm Q}+2\eta\operatorname{pop}(q))U]\\
 &+H_rsT
 +H_{r+1}s^2[T-d_{\rm Q}+2\eta-\eta N]
 +H_rs^3[T-2d_{\rm Q}+2\eta-\eta N].
\end{split}
\tag{4.8}
\]

For each quarter interior \(k=qL_q+j\), \(j<L_q-1\),
\(\sigma_k=s^q\sigma_j\) and
\(k\mathbin{\rm xor}i=(q+1)L_q-1-j\). This proves its contribution in
(4.8). The three bridges \(qL_q-1\), \(q=1,2,3\), have xor values
\(0,L_q,2L_q\) against \(i\); the first bridge is an actual diagonal and
contributes \(T\). In particular its \(\eta N\) correction is not lost.
Thus \(E_i=\langle P_n,w_i\rangle\), and the complete pair constants are

\[
\begin{gathered}
 K_i=sJ_i+s^2T,\quad K_n=tJ_n+t^2T,\quad
 c_n=tJ_n+\tfrac12t^2T,\\
 C_{in}=tJ_{\rm pre}-sJ_i+stB-s^2T,\qquad
 C_{ni}=sE_i-tJ_n+stB-t^2T.
\end{gathered}
\tag{4.9}
\]

No two-step trajectory replaces these inherited prefixes.
For pair potentials \(u,v\), the retained domain is exactly

\[
 u\ge K_i,\quad v\ge K_n,\quad v-u\le C_{in},\quad u-v\le C_{ni}.
\tag{4.10}
\]

For \(p=s^2T,q=t^2T,b=stB<0\), its closest-two-halfspace radius squared is

\[
 r_{\rm pair}(u,v)=
 \min_{d_1\ge u,d_2\ge v}
 \frac{qd_1^2-2bd_1d_2+pd_2^2}{pq-b^2}.
\tag{4.11}
\]

This follows by minimizing the norm in the span of the two independent
vertices; an orthogonal component only increases the norm. The active
cases are unchanged: zero at \(d=(0,0)\) if \(u,v\le0\); \(u^2/p\) at
\((u,bu/p)\) if \(u>0,v\le bu/p\); \(v^2/q\) at \((bv/q,v)\) if
\(v>0,u\le bv/q\); otherwise both faces are active at \((u,v)\).
Free-coordinate minimization and the remaining nonnegative derivatives
prove these exhaustive cases, with matching boundary values.

The pair numerator \(2(v-c_n)\) is at least \(t^2T>0\) on (4.10).
The positive certificate in Section 8 excludes zero pair radius.
Removing the other halfspaces can only reduce the full radius, so an
upper bound using (4.11) is an upper bound for every full legal initializer.

## 5. Exact propagation of the source correction

This section uses a tilde only to identify the old defective expressions,
not to propose another initializer, radius law, or mechanism. Let
\(\widetilde\sigma_k=x^{\lceil k/2\rceil}y^{\lfloor(k+1)/4\rfloor}\).
Then \(\sigma_k=\gamma_k\widetilde\sigma_k\), with \(\gamma_k=y\)
at \(k=2\pmod4\) and \(\gamma_k=1\) otherwise. In particular, if a full
matrix were assembled from that erroneous formula, its corrections would be

\[
 V=\widetilde V\Gamma,\quad G=\Gamma\widetilde G\Gamma,\qquad
 A_{k\ell}=\gamma_\ell\sum_{b<k}h_b\gamma_b\widetilde G_{b\ell},
 \quad\Gamma=\operatorname{diag}(\gamma_k).
\tag{5.1}
\]

All full \(C,K,c_n\) must consequently be recomputed from (3.1).
The unscaled kernel \(B\), physical word, roots, \(x,y,\theta\), and
bridge radii are unchanged.

For an expression \(Q\), write \(\Delta Q=Q-\widetilde Q\).
The exact polynomial correction is

\[
 \Delta{\cal P}_j=(y-1)xH_0z^3w
      \prod_{b=0}^{j-3}[1+w(\theta z^4)^{2^b}].
\tag{5.2}
\]

Let \(L_j=2^{j-2}\) and \(d_*=x(1-y)H_0>0\). The finite sums therefore obey

\[
\begin{split}
 \Delta U_j&=-d_*\sum_{q=0}^{L_j-1}\theta^q
            =-d_*\frac{1-\theta^{L_j}}{1-\theta},\\
 \Delta M_j&=-d_*\sum_{q=0}^{L_j-1}(4q+2)\theta^q,\\
 \Delta Z_j&=-d_*\sum_{q=0}^{L_j-1}
                       (\operatorname{pop}(q)+1)\theta^q.
\end{split}
\tag{5.3}
\]

The exact downstream corrections, with depth \(r\) for unindexed sums, are

\[
\begin{gathered}
 \Delta J_i=b_i\Delta U+\frac{2\Delta M}{N-1}-2\eta\Delta Z,\qquad
 \Delta J_{\rm pre}=b_n\Delta U+\frac{2\Delta M}{N-1}-2\eta\Delta Z,\\
 \Delta J_n=b_n\Delta U_m+\frac{2\Delta M_m}{N-1}-2\eta\Delta Z_m,\\
 \Delta E_i=\sum_{q=0}^{3}s^q
     [\Delta J_i+(-qd_{\rm Q}+2\eta\operatorname{pop}(q))\Delta U],\\
 \Delta K_i=s\Delta J_i,\quad
 \Delta K_n=t\Delta J_n,\quad \Delta c_n=t\Delta J_n,\\
 \Delta C_{in}=t\Delta J_{\rm pre}-s\Delta J_i,\qquad
 \Delta C_{ni}=s\Delta E_i-t\Delta J_n.
\end{gathered}
\tag{5.4}
\]

The pair Gram matrix stays unchanged, but its forward and reverse row
constants do not. No sign for \(\Delta J_i,\Delta J_{\rm pre},\Delta J_n\)
or their certificate combination is assumed: their kernels are signed.
For the \(J\) and \(\kappa\) used below, the exact propagation is

\[
 \Delta J=\Delta J_i-s^3\Delta J_{\rm pre}+(1+s^3)\Delta J_n,\qquad
 \Delta\kappa=\ell s\,\Delta J,\quad \ell=\frac{2t}{s+t}.
\tag{5.5}
\]

Thus the old prefix expressions cannot simply be reused as their original
values. Sections 6-8 prove the required inequalities directly for the
corrected quantities.

## 6. Damping-four radial estimates and the repaired ratio table

The elementary bounds needed below are

\[
 N\ge2^{16},\quad K_{\rm mix}\ge N/2,\quad
 \frac{12}5<c<\frac{121}{50},\quad
 \frac25<a<\frac5{12},\quad
 \beta>\frac{999}{1000},\quad
 \frac43<T<\frac{27}{20}.
\tag{6.1}
\]

Use \(7/5<\sqrt2<17/12\), \(\epsilon<1/6000\), and
\(\beta>999/1000\) for these parameter bounds. The bounds on \(N\) and
\(K_{\rm mix}\) follow by doubling the elementary inequality
\(2^m\ge2m+2\). In particular \(\eta\le2/N^3\).

For \(\ell=0,1\), write \(H_\ell=1+b\), where \(b=a,\beta\). The root
equation gives

\[
 (1-\rho_\ell)(\rho_\ell+b)
       =\frac{2(1-b)E_\ell\rho_\ell}{T}.
\tag{6.2}
\]

The quadratic has negative constant term and positive value at one;
thus \(0<\rho_\ell<1\), and
\(\rho_\ell/(\rho_\ell+b)\le1/(1+b)\).
The exact energies give
\(NE_0<1+3/N\), \(NE_1<4\). Consequently

\[
 N(1-\rho_0)<\frac9{14}(1+3/N)<\frac{13}{20}<\frac23,
 \qquad N(1-\rho_1)<6\epsilon<\frac1{1000}.
\tag{6.3}
\]

For the first inequality use \(a>2/5,T>4/3\); for the second use
\(1-\beta=\epsilon\). Conversely, \(\rho_0>999/1000\),
\(1-a>7/12\), \(\rho_0+a<17/12\), \(NE_0>1\), and \(T<27/20\) in
(6.2) yield

\[
 N(1-\rho_0)>\frac{280}{459}\frac{999}{1000}>\frac{121}{200}.
\tag{6.4}
\]

Besides the old bounds, this proves the needed ordering
\(\rho_0<\rho_1\), hence \(0<x<y<1\).
For the unchanged damping factor four, (6.3)-(6.4) give

\[
 \frac{23}5<N(1-x)<\frac{93}{20}<5,\qquad
 4<N(1-y)<\frac{4001}{1000}<5.
\tag{6.5}
\]

For the lower bound on \(N(1-x)\), use
\(N(1-x)=N(1-\rho_0)+4\rho_0>
4+121/200-8/(3N)>23/5\).

Set \(\zeta=-(N/4)\log\theta\).
Integrating \(1/(1-u)\) gives
\(u\le-\log(1-u)\le u/(1-u)\), so

\[
 \frac{33}{10}<\zeta<
 \frac{13301}{4000(1-5/N)}<\frac{10}{3}.
\tag{6.6}
\]

The last comparison requires \(N>200000/97\), which holds here.
By (4.1), \(s=e^{-\zeta/4}\), \(t=e^{-\zeta}\), and

\[
 \frac37<s<\frac49,\qquad t<\frac1{27}.
\tag{6.7}
\]

Here are finite rational exponential checks. For \(0<u<3\), the
exponential tail from degree two is at most
\((u^2/2)/(1-u/3)\). Hence
\(e^{5/6}\le361/156<7/3\).
The first four terms of \(e^{33/40}\) sum to
\(867417/384000>9/4\).
The first six terms of \(e^{11/10}\) sum to
\(36015101/12000000>3\); cubing gives \(e^{33/10}>27\).
These prove (6.7) without numerical evaluation.

For \(u_k=(k+1)/N\), the corrected exact ratio table is

| \(k\bmod4\) | \(\sigma_k/e^{-\zeta u_k}\) |
|---|---|
| 0 | \(\theta^{-1/4}\) |
| 1 | \(y^{-1/2}\) |
| 2 | \(x^{-1/2}y^{1/4}\) |
| 3 | \(1\) |

The third entry replaces \(x^{-1/2}y^{-3/4}\).
Write \(X=-\log x,Y=-\log y\). Equations (6.3)-(6.5) give
\(0<Y<X<6/N\). The four logarithms are
\((2X+Y)/4,\ Y/2,\ X/2-Y/4,\ 0\).
Each is nonnegative; the third is greater than \(X/4>0\).
All are less than \(9/(2N)\), and hence also less than \(15/(2N)\).
Using \(e^u\le1/(1-u)\) and \(N>120\) gives the same envelope as the
old proof, now for the actual chronological radii:

\[
 \boxed{0\le\sigma_k-e^{-\zeta u_k}<8/N.}
\tag{6.8}
\]

At each actual bridge \(k=qL_q-1\), the ratio is exactly one and
\(\sigma_k=s^q\). Also \(\sigma_k\le1\). This repairs the decisive
dependency rather than importing a changed-damping bound.

## 7. Corrected physical prefix bound

Write \(S=S_r\). The original word has four quarter interiors and three
bridges, so

\[
 S_m=4S+2H_r+H_{r+1},\qquad
 D_m=8S+4H_r+2H_{r+1}+1.
\tag{7.1}
\]

The following bounds involve only the physical word:

\[
 H_r<S/2,\quad H_{r+1}>(9/10)S,\quad D_m<12S,\quad S_m<6S.
\tag{7.2}
\]

To check them, put \(\lambda_0=1+\sqrt2\). Direct expansion gives

\[
 c(c-2)-\beta=(2\lambda_0^2-2\lambda_0+1)\epsilon
                  +\lambda_0^2\epsilon^2>c\epsilon.
\]

Its linear coefficient equals \(2\lambda_0+3>7\), while \(c<5/2\).
Thus \(c-2-a>\epsilon\) and \(0<a/(c-2)<1\). Formula (2.2) gives
\(S<c^r\), so \(H_{r+1}=1+\beta c^r>\beta S>(9/10)S\).
The bound \(L_q>8m\) holds at \(m=16\) and persists under doubling.
Therefore

\[
 H_r=(c-2)S-(c-2-a)L_q+c-1
       <(c-2)S<(21/50)S<S/2.
\]

Here \((c-2-a)L_q>2>c-1\). Moreover
\(H_{r+1}=cH_r+1-c<(2541/2500)S\).
Since \(S>L_q-1>4\), (7.1) gives
\(D_m/S<29282/2500+1/4<12\), and then \(S_m<6S\).

The scalar paying the same original pair certificate is

\[
 J=\langle P_i,w_i+w_n\rangle
       +(1+s^3)\langle P_n-P_i,w_n\rangle
   =J_i+J_{\rm pre}+(1+s^3)(J_n-J_{\rm pre}).
\tag{7.3}
\]

We now prove directly for the corrected prefixes that

\[
 \boxed{J>(7/15)S.}
\tag{7.4}
\]

From the full kernel (2.3), for \(k<n\),

\[
 |B_{kn}-(-2/3+2u_k)|<3/N,
\tag{7.5}
\]

and for \(k<i\),

\[
 |B_{ki}-(5/6+2u_k)|<3/N.
\tag{7.6}
\]

The exact error in (7.5) is
\(1/N^2-2(1-u_k)/(N-1)+
\eta[2(m-\operatorname{pop}(k))-N]\).
For (7.6) it is
\(1/N^2-2(1/4-u_k)/(N-1)+
\eta[2(r-\operatorname{pop}(k))-N]\).
The applicable popcounts are at most \(m\) and \(r\); using
\(2m<N\), their absolute errors are at most
\(3/N^2+2/(N-1)<3/N\). All tiny characters have been included.

Define

\[
 f(u)=e^{-\zeta u}(1/6+4u),\quad 0\le u\le1/4,\qquad
 g(u)=e^{-\zeta u}(-2/3+2u),\quad 1/4\le u\le1.
\]

Let \(J_{\rm ideal}\) be the literal physical \(h_k\)-weighted sum of
\(f(u_k)\) for \(k<i\), plus \(1+s^3\) times the literal weighted sum
of \(g(u_k)\) for \(i\le k<n\).
By (6.8), (7.5)-(7.6), and \(\sigma_k\le1\), the first integrand error
is less than \(6/N+(8/N)(7/6)<16/N\); the second is less than
\(3/N+(8/N)(4/3)<16/N\). Thus

\[
 |J-J_{\rm ideal}|<32S_m/N<192S/N<256S/N.
\tag{7.7}
\]

This is an absolute error estimate on signed sums. It covers every
chronological step, including the negative early part of the tail.

Each quarter interior \(k=qL_q+j\), \(0\le j<L_q-1\), has total
physical weight \(S\) and \(h\)-weighted mean time \((q+1/2)/4\).
Indeed \(j+1\) and \(L_q-(j+1)\) have equal two-adic valuation, hence
equal physical step weights and mean \(L_q/2\). This identity uses the
clock only; it does not reset any state or radius.

The second derivatives are

\[
 f''(u)=\zeta e^{-\zeta u}[\zeta(1/6+4u)-8]<0
                     \quad(0\le u\le1/4),
\]
\[
 g''(u)=\zeta e^{-\zeta u}[\zeta(-2/3+2u)-4]<0
                     \quad(1/4\le u\le3/4).
\]

Use \(\zeta<10/3\); the corresponding bracketed upper bounds are
\(35/9-8<0\) and \(25/9-4<0\).
A concave function lies above its endpoint chord. Using the exact means,
the first three quarter contributions are bounded below by \(S\) times

\[
 (1+7s)/12,\qquad (-s+2s^2)/12,\qquad (2s^2+5s^3)/12.
\tag{7.8}
\]

In the fourth quarter, \(g(u)\ge5s^4/6\), since its affine factor is at
least \(5/6\) and its exponential factor at least \(s^4\).
The three actual bridge contributions to the ideal tail are exactly

\[
 H_r(-s+5s^3)/6+H_{r+1}s^2/3.
\tag{7.9}
\]

The first coefficient is negative, because \(s<4/9\) gives
\(5s^2<80/81<1\). Therefore the upper bound \(H_r<S/2\), with its
inequality direction reversed upon multiplication by that coefficient,
and the lower bound \(H_{r+1}>(9/10)S\) give the total tail lower bound
\(S\,B_{\rm fun}(s)\), where

\[
 B_{\rm fun}(s)=-s/6+19s^2/30+5s^3/6+5s^4/6.
\tag{7.10}
\]

For \(s\ge3/7\), its derivative is positive: already \(19s/15>1/6\),
and the other derivative terms are nonnegative. Direct rational
substitution in this displayed finite polynomial gives

\[
 B_{\rm fun}(s)>B_{\rm fun}(3/7)=1664/12005>2/15.
\]

The ideal tail is consequently positive. Its prefactor \(1+s^3\)
may now be replaced by one in a lower bound. The first-quarter lower
bound in (7.8) is greater than \(S/3\), yielding

\[
 J_{\rm ideal}/S>1/3+1664/12005=7/15+38/7203.
\]

Finally \(256/N\le1/256<1/200<38/7203\). Subtract (7.7) to obtain
(7.4). This checks every quantitative estimate used to pay the certificate.
The reverse row requires its exact formula (4.8)-(4.9), but no numerical
lower or upper estimate for it is used: its multiplier below is zero.

## 8. The unchanged positive multiplier and the restored constant

First derive the weak bound with its signs. For nonnegative pair
multipliers \(\lambda_{k\ell}\) and zero-row multipliers \(\nu_k\), put

\[
 q_{\rm dual}=2e_n+\operatorname{out}(\lambda)
                         -\operatorname{in}(\lambda)+\nu,\qquad
 \kappa=2c_n-\sum\lambda_{k\ell}C_{k\ell}+\sum\nu_kK_k.
\]

If \(q_{\rm dual}\ge0\) and \(\kappa>0\), the constraints give

\[
\begin{split}
 q_{\rm dual}^Tp-\kappa
 ={}&2(p_n-c_n)
  +\sum\lambda_{k\ell}[C_{k\ell}-(p_\ell-p_k)]\\
 &+\sum\nu_k(p_k-K_k)\ \ge\ 2(p_n-c_n).
\end{split}
\tag{8.1}
\]

The vector \(Vq_{\rm dual}\) lies in the positive cone. For the closest
polar point \(z_\star\),
\(q_{\rm dual}^Tp\le\langle z_0-z_\star,Vq_{\rm dual}\rangle
\le R_{\rm true}\|Vq_{\rm dual}\|\).
Completing the square in \(1/R_{\rm true}\) proves

\[
 \frac{2(p_n-c_n)}{R_{\rm true}^2}
 \le\frac{q_{\rm dual}^TGq_{\rm dual}}{4\kappa}.
\tag{8.2}
\]

The same proof applies to the pair halfspaces and radius (4.11);
at radius zero, (8.1) would contradict its positive numerator, as claimed.
In the perspective, the objective is at most
\(\|Vq_{\rm dual}\|\sqrt{\tau}-\kappa\tau\), giving the same bound also
at its zero point. No strong duality is invoked.

Preserve exactly the old sole nonzero multiplier,

\[
 \lambda_{in}=\ell=\frac{2t}{s+t},\qquad
 \lambda_{ni}=0,\qquad \nu_i=\nu_n=0.
\tag{8.3}
\]

The two coordinates of \(q_{\rm dual}\) are \(\ell,2-\ell\), both
positive. The exact angle, including every character, gives

\[
 V_{\rm pair}q_{\rm dual}=\frac{2st}{s+t}(w_i+w_n),\qquad
 q_{\rm dual}^TG_{\rm pair}q_{\rm dual}
       =\frac{8s^2t^2(T+B)}{(s+t)^2}.
\tag{8.4}
\]

Using the corrected constants (4.9) and the corrected \(J\) of (7.3),
direct expansion, without discarding the terminal half-square, gives

\[
 \kappa=2c_n-\ell C_{in}
 =\ell s\left[
 J+sT-tB+\frac{(s+t)tT}{2s}\right]
 >\ell sJ=\frac{2stJ}{s+t}>0.
\tag{8.5}
\]

Every omitted term is positive because \(B<0\) and \(s,t,T>0\).
Equations (8.2), (8.4)-(8.5) bound every score in the pair relaxation,
and hence every legal full initializer's score, strictly by

\[
 \frac{t(T+B)}{(1+s^3)J}.
\tag{8.6}
\]

All bounds used next have been rederived for the same damping-four law:
\(t<1/27\), \(T+B<7/6\), \(1+s^3>370/343\),
\(J>(7/15)S\), and \(D_m<12S\). Consequently

\[
 \frac{t(T+B)}{(1+s^3)J}
 <\frac1{27}\frac76\frac{343}{370}\frac{15}{7S}
 =\frac{343}{3996S}
 <\frac{343}{333D_m}<\frac{17}{16D_m}.
\tag{8.7}
\]

The last comparison is \(5488<5661\).
For fixed \(m\), (8.2) has one constant independent of the initializer,
and that constant is strictly below (8.7); thus the supremum, including
its adjoined zero, also obeys (1.1). If a smoothness constant \(L>0\)
is desired, \(L\Phi\) with steps \(h_k/L\) gives the same points and
normalized score.

## 9. Source-only impact audit of the public numerical construction

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002484 -->
# All-initializer vector perspective, frozen before computation

Status: independently derived, source-bound research draft; not submitted to
the verifier. Allocation cae5eb96abab4e71, one direct gpt-6-astra/ultra call.
The original minimax problem remains OPEN.

## Sources and scope

The governing TASK has SHA256
3e81235f10352bec30621f084546295abfb9438e1de03f6215ecd26aca188db4.
Public source `../shared_evidence/a37b81ea81b53910.md`, Sections 2--3,
supplies the original vertices, chronological count and initializer interface.
The following derivation independently establishes the vector formulation,
without importing that source's unverified asymptotic estimates. The old
`../gray_shift_payoff_discovery_20261003/HANDOFF.md` is scope evidence only:
that batch varied a common potential shift. No old arrays or programs are used.
The erroneous old damping-four proof and its ongoing repair are not premises.
All cited input bytes are fixed by FREEZE.json before any mathematical child.

## Exact finite object and every original normal

Fix n+1 linearly independent nonzero vectors v_0,...,v_n in R^(n+1),
nonnegative h_0,...,h_(n-1), and V with those vectors as columns. Let

    D0=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}, Kpolar={z:V^T z<=0},
    Phi(x)=max_(v in D0) [<x,v>-||v||^2/2],
    P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k, A_ij=<P_i,v_j>, G_ij=<v_i,v_j>,
    C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+G_ij-G_ii,
    K_i=A_ii+G_ii, c_n=A_nn+G_nn/2.

Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer as the Euclidean
projection p(x) of x onto D0. Its normal condition is
<x-p(x),u-p(x)><=0 for every u in D0. Necessity follows by taking a
one-sided derivative along each segment; sufficiency follows by expanding
||x-u||^2. Checking the generating vertices, including zero, suffices.
The two projection normals imply
||p(x)-p(y)||^2<=<x-y,p(x)-p(y)> and hence p is 1-Lipschitz.
The maximizing formula gives the lower affine support with gradient p(x);
using p(x+e) for the upper comparison gives an error of at most ||e||^2.
Thus Phi is convex, differentiable, has gradient p, and is 1-smooth.
Since zero is a candidate, Phi>=0. Phi(x)=0 iff V^T x<=0: the forward
implication follows by considering arbitrarily small positive multiples of
each v_i. Thus its whole minimizer set is Kpolar.

For an initializer x0 let f=V^T x0 (initializer potentials, not function
values) and x_i=x0-P_i. The normal to v_i against v_j is exactly

    <x_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>=f_j-f_i-C_ij <=0.

The normal against zero is K_i-f_i<=0. Consequently every ordered pair
f_j-f_i<=C_ij and every zero row f_i>=K_i are jointly necessary and
sufficient for all prescribed vertices, terminal included, to be the actual
gradients along this GD transcript. There is no h_n. In that case

    Phi(x_i)=f_i-A_ii-G_ii/2,
    q(x0):=2 Phi(x_n)=2(f_n-c_n)>=G_nn>0.

Let r(x0)=dist(x0,Kpolar)^2. A closest point exists: Kpolar is nonempty
and closed, and a minimizing sequence can be restricted to a bounded ball.
The zero row at i=0 gives <x0,v0>>=||v0||^2, while every polar point has
nonpositive pairing; therefore r(x0)>=||v0||^2>0 for a legal initializer.
The closest-minimum normalized score is Q(x0)=q(x0)/r(x0), in W=2R
normalization. All initializer potentials are represented because V^T is
invertible. The numerical implementation never forms an inverse Gram.

## Exact perspective and both directions

Variables b,zstar in R^(n+1) and tau>=0 define F=V^T b. Consider

    maximize O=2 F_n-2 tau c_n,
    F_j-F_i<=tau C_ij for EVERY i,j,
    F_i>=tau K_i, V^T zstar<=0, ||b-zstar||^2<=tau.

The last inequality is represented exactly by the ordinary SOC

    ||(2(b-zstar),tau-1)|| <= tau+1,

with the explicit tau>=0 constraint retained. Squaring both sides yields
4||b-zstar||^2<=4tau, and the nonnegative right side makes this reversible.

Given any legal x0, choose its actual closest polar point y and set
tau=1/r(x0), b=tau x0, zstar=tau y. Homogeneity of the polar cone and
of the normal inequalities makes this a feasible perspective point.
The cone is tight and its objective equals Q(x0). Thus the perspective
supremum is at least the supremum over every legal initializer.

Conversely, any exactly feasible point with tau>0 gives x0=b/tau and
y=zstar/tau in Kpolar. Dividing the original rows proves full legality.
Its selected radius Rsel=||x0-y||^2<=1/tau, while r(x0)<=Rsel. Its
positive terminal numerator is q=O/tau. Hence

    Q(x0)=q/r(x0) >= Qsel=q/Rsel >= O.

This proves equality of the two suprema when the legal set is nonempty;
it does not assert that an arbitrary feasible zstar is a closest point.
At an attained positive optimum, failure of either distance equality would
allow mapping the same initializer back using its closest point to produce
a strictly larger objective. This analytical observation is not a license
to declare floating output exactly optimal or its returned zstar closest.

At tau=0 the cone forces b=zstar. Zero rows force F>=0, while polar rows
force F<=0, so F=0; independence gives b=zstar=0 and O=0. The zero point
is always feasible. Therefore the program value is precisely the supremum
of {0} union all legal initializer scores, even if the legal set is empty.
The conventional supremum of an empty legal set is not this zero value.

For floating output, O, Qsel, and a claim about Q must stay separate.
Positive numerical residuals invalidate an exact feasibility assertion;
no returned ratio becomes a rigorous lower bound without repairing every
normal and polar defect by an independently authorized exact argument.

## Two known calibrations

1. Empty word, v0=1: P=A=C=0, G=K=1 and c=1/2. Here F=b,
   b>=tau, zstar<=0 and (b-zstar)^2<=tau. Thus b>=0, tau>=b^2 and
   O=2b-tau<=2b-b^2<=1. At tau=b=1,zstar=0 every original row and
   cone hold and O=1. This is the exact optimum, also Q=1.
2. h=(1), v0=(1,0),v1=(0,1): G=I, P0=0,P1=v0,
   A=[[0,0],[1,0]], C=[[0,-1],[0,0]], K=(1,1), c=1/2.
   The ordered rows give F1-F0<=-tau and F0-F1<=0; their sum forces
   tau=0. The preceding zero-scale argument forces b=zstar=0, so O=0.
   There is no legal positive-scale initializer, despite program feasibility.
Both calibrations use the same matrix/prefix/constraint builder and one solve.

## Frozen finite Gray construction

Depth j is 4,5,6; N=2^j, n=N-1. The physical enclosing parameter is
M=1536 in every production case. Define eps=1/(4M), beta=1-eps,
c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c, H_l=1+a c^l,
h_k=H_(nu2(k+1)). Let Gray(k)=k xor floor(k/2). The N rows of w_k
consist of characters chi_t(Gray(k)) with all nonzero t=1,...,N-1,
followed by the constant coordinate 1/sqrt(3). The j distinguished
characters t_l=N-2^l have squared amplitude 2^l/(N-1); every other
nonzero character has squared amplitude eta=1/[N^2(N-1-j)]. Thus
Tframe=4/3+1/N^2 and direct character summation gives

    B_kl=Tframe-2(k xor l)/(N-1)+2eta popcount(k xor l)
          -eta N+eta N 1_(k=l).

The implementation constructs characters first and independently exports
every raw signed discrepancy between their inner products and this kernel.
Walsh orthogonality with all positive amplitudes, including the vertical
row, gives independence; multiplying columns by positive sigma preserves it.
For each level let E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta(N/2-l-1),
Badj_l=Tframe-2E_l. Let rho_l be the positive root of
rho^2+(H_l-2)(Badj_l/Tframe)rho+1-H_l=0. The constant term is negative
since H_l>1, so exactly one root is positive. No numerical root is optimized.
For damping delta, r0=rho0(1-delta/N), r1=rho1(1-delta/N), other r_l=1.
All selected N exceed delta. Sigma is constructed chronologically from
sigma0=1 and sigma_(k+1)=sigma_k r_(nu2(k+1)), then v_k=sigma_k w_k.

Among integers 1,...,k, there are ceil(k/2) odd integers and
floor((k+2)/4) integers of valuation one (2,6,10,...). Hence, writing
x=r0,y=r1, the independent closed expression is
sigma_k=x^ceil(k/2)y^floor((k+2)/4). In particular sigma2=x*y.
Every signed chronological-minus-closed floating discrepancy is retained.
No use is made of the old erroneous floor((k+1)/4) count.

Each finite depth changes the character dictionary, eta, weights, and
radial law. These are not submatrices of the full 2^1536 construction.
D_j=1+2 sum h_k and D_j O are finite discovery diagnostics only.

## Frozen execution and reporting semantics

CASES.json fixes six jobs in order: the two calibrations, then
(N,delta)=(16,2),(32,2),(64,2),(64,4). The final case is only a control.
The stdlib broker precharges 20 seconds per attempted job and starts one
new process group via the existing public launcher. It waits at most 20
seconds measured from immediately before Popen, kills the whole group on
timeout, records every outcome, and never retries. Either calibration
failure retires all unlaunched slots. All imports, construction, one
CLARABEL solve, residuals, transcript extraction and exports occur inside
the timed child. No numerical setup or array reanalysis occurs in the broker.

Solver options are max_iter=120, max_threads=1, time_limit=10.0,
tol_gap_abs=tol_gap_rel=tol_feas=1e-9, warm_start=False. No fallback,
secondary projection, inverse Gram, graph algorithm or data reuse occurs.
Calibration passes require optimal status, absolute objective error<=1e-7,
all original scaled signed residuals<=1e-7, SOC and squared-cone signed
residuals<=1e-7, and complete exports; calibration two also |tau|<=1e-7.
The exact construction fixture discrepancies must be zero as an additional
builder check. Every residual is exported with its sign, including positive
defects; the tolerance classification never alters the values.

For extraction only, robust positivity means finite tau>1e-7 and
tau>100*abs(||b-zstar||^2-tau). This is a frozen numerical division guard,
not an exact positivity proof. When it passes, save x0=b/tau, y=zstar/tau,
all prescribed x_i=x0-P_i and v_i, all unscaled pair/zero normals, all
polar defects, terminal numerator, selected squared radius, selected score,
and raw recurrence discrepancies. The transcript is the actual prescribed
recursion; its identification with GD of Phi remains conditional on exact
normal feasibility. No second projection solve is performed. True closest
distance is explicitly uncomputed. Numerical KKT defects and dual values
may be recorded but never certify a closest point or exact feasibility.

After closure only saved-text reads/transcription, reports and byte hashes
are allowed. The grant does not authorize a post-freeze edit, replacement,
further mathematical child, formal call, truth-store write or renewal.

# Handoff: frozen full-initializer batch closed without execution

Allocation `cae5eb96abab4e71`; one supplied gpt-6-astra/ultra invocation.
Original minimax Goal OPEN. This is an unverified analytical draft and an
administrative failure receipt, not a numerical or verifier result.

Read `REPORT.md` first and frozen `DERIVATION.md` for the complete interface.
For V with independent columns, P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k, A=P^T V,
G=V^T V, C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+G_ij-G_ii, K_i=A_ii+G_ii and
c_n=A_nn+G_nn/2, the vector program is

    sup 2(V^T b)_n-2tau c_n,
    (V^T b)_j-(V^T b)_i<=tau C_ij for every ordered i,j,
    V^T b>=tau K, V^T zstar<=0, ||b-zstar||^2<=tau, tau>=0.

For exact tau>0, x0=b/tau and y=zstar/tau give a legal initializer and
selected polar point; true closest-minimum score >= selected-polar score >=
program objective. Conversely any legal initializer with its actual closest
polar point maps to an equal objective. The program supremum equals the
zero-adjoined supremum over all legal initializers. At tau=0 all variables
are zero. Therefore a zero optimum can accompany an EMPTY legal set.
These statements are derived in the frozen draft, not verifier-accepted.

The empty word with v0=1 has exact optimum 1. The h=(1), V=I2 fixture
has C01=-1,C10=0, so no positive-tau point and zero-adjoined optimum 0.
These two analytical fixtures were not executed. The intended production
cases (N,delta)=(16,2),(32,2),(64,2),(64,4), physical M=1536, also did not run.

At the single broker launch, preflight detected different host/container

# Full-initializer finite Gray perspective discovery

ONE new native invocation, gpt-6-astra/ultra, consultOFF, original Goal OPEN. The application Goal resumed on2026-10-08; OPERATOR.md records live active evidence. All old numerical grants are CLOSED. This NEW grant is exactly SIX jobs/120 ACCOUNTED seconds: two calibrations plus four fixed finite production cases, external20-second process-group limit and full20-second precharge per job. Before running mathematics, independently derive the vector form of the exact zero-adjoined perspective. For fixed linearly independent vertices v_i, i=0..n, let Vmat have those columns, P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k, A_ij=<P_i,v_j>, G_ij=<v_i,v_j>, C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+G_ij-G_ii, K_i=A_ii+G_ii, c_n=A_nn+G_nn/2. Variables are b,zstar in R^(n+1), tau>=0, and F=Vmat^T b. Maximize
    2F_n-2tau*c_n
subject to EVERY original ordered constraint F_j-F_i<=tau*C_ij,
    F_i>=tau*K_i, Vmat^T zstar<=0, ||b-zstar||^2<=tau.
Use an exact SOC representation if convenient. Do NOT invert G numerically. Derive both directions to the legal-initializer score and the tau=0/empty-set caveat. For tau>0, x0=b/tau and selected polar point zstar/tau produce an original transcript; the program maximizes the exact full closest-minimum ratio at the analytical level. An arbitrary returned polar point is not automatically closest. Export the program value separately from any selected-minimum ratio and preserve this distinction.

Construct production vertices from the ORIGINAL definition, not the wrong count. There are case depth j and N=2^j,n=N-1. The physical step parameter is FIXED enclosing M=1536:
 eps=1/(4M),beta=1-eps,c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps),a=beta/c,
 h_k=1+a*c^nu2(k+1),0<=k<n.
For every nonzero binary character t in1..N-1 let chi_t(u)=(-1)^popcount(t&u), Gray(k)=k xor floor(k/2). Distinguished characters are t_l=N-2^l,0<=l<j, with squared weights2^l/(N-1). EVERY other nonzero character has squared weight eta=1/[N^2(N-1-j)]. Add the constant vertical coordinate1/sqrt3. Let w_k be this full N-dimensional frame, Tframe=4/3+1/N^2. The exact kernel is Tframe-2*(k xor l)/(N-1)+2eta*popcount(k xor l)-eta*N+eta*N*1_(k=l).

For level l define H_l=1+a*c^l,E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta*(N/2-l-1),Badj_l=Tframe-2E_l. rho_l is the POSITIVE root of rho^2+(H_l-2)*(Badj_l/Tframe)*rho+1-H_l=0. Fix damping delta_case as specified below; r0=rho0*(1-delta_case/N),r1=rho1*(1-delta_case/N),r_l=1 for l>=2. Build sigma_0=1 and sigma_(k+1)=sigma_k*r_(nu2(k+1)) by ACTUAL CHRONOLOGICAL multiplication. Within the timed child also compare to x^ceil(k/2)*y^floor((k+2)/4), retaining raw rounding differences. In particular sigma_2 must be x*y, not x. Vertices v_k=sigma_k*w_k. No weight, root, radial ratio or physical step is optimized or repaired.

The SIX frozen jobs, in this exact order:

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002488 -->
# All-initializer vector perspective, frozen before computation

Status: independently derived, source-bound research draft; not submitted to
the verifier. Allocation 287b7a3644e5bda3, resume 5b362b0a381a2444,
one direct gpt-6-astra/ultra call.
The original minimax problem remains OPEN.

## Sources and scope

The governing TASK has SHA256
2d84dba65bbeb92d696629972ff5daf98b8effae6557ba4298fed7b6334b9e54.
Public source `../shared_evidence/a37b81ea81b53910.md`, Sections 2--3,
supplies the original vertices, chronological count and initializer interface.
The prior closed snapshot packet supplies the vector formulation and builder.
This packet rechecks that formulation and independently derives the positive
calibration below before freezing or executing any mathematical child.
The following derivation establishes the vector formulation,
without importing that source's unverified asymptotic estimates. The old
`../gray_shift_payoff_discovery_20261003/HANDOFF.md` is scope evidence only:
that batch varied a common potential shift. No old arrays or programs are used.
The erroneous old damping-four proof and its ongoing repair are not premises.
All cited input bytes are fixed by FREEZE.json before any mathematical child.

## Exact finite object and every original normal

Fix n+1 linearly independent nonzero vectors v_0,...,v_n in R^(n+1),
nonnegative h_0,...,h_(n-1), and V with those vectors as columns. Let

    D0=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}, Kpolar={z:V^T z<=0},
    Phi(x)=max_(v in D0) [<x,v>-||v||^2/2],
    P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k, A_ij=<P_i,v_j>, G_ij=<v_i,v_j>,
    C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+G_ij-G_ii,
    K_i=A_ii+G_ii, c_n=A_nn+G_nn/2.

Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer as the Euclidean
projection p(x) of x onto D0. Its normal condition is
<x-p(x),u-p(x)><=0 for every u in D0. Necessity follows by taking a
one-sided derivative along each segment; sufficiency follows by expanding
||x-u||^2. Checking the generating vertices, including zero, suffices.
The two projection normals imply
||p(x)-p(y)||^2<=<x-y,p(x)-p(y)> and hence p is 1-Lipschitz.
The maximizing formula gives the lower affine support with gradient p(x);
using p(x+e) for the upper comparison gives an error of at most ||e||^2.
Thus Phi is convex, differentiable, has gradient p, and is 1-smooth.
Since zero is a candidate, Phi>=0. Phi(x)=0 iff V^T x<=0: the forward
implication follows by considering arbitrarily small positive multiples of
each v_i. Thus its whole minimizer set is Kpolar.

For an initializer x0 let f=V^T x0 (initializer potentials, not function
values) and x_i=x0-P_i. The normal to v_i against v_j is exactly

    <x_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>=f_j-f_i-C_ij <=0.

The normal against zero is K_i-f_i<=0. Consequently every ordered pair
f_j-f_i<=C_ij and every zero row f_i>=K_i are jointly necessary and
sufficient for all prescribed vertices, terminal included, to be the actual
gradients along this GD transcript. There is no h_n. In that case

    Phi(x_i)=f_i-A_ii-G_ii/2,
    q(x0):=2 Phi(x_n)=2(f_n-c_n)>=G_nn>0.

Let r(x0)=dist(x0,Kpolar)^2. A closest point exists: Kpolar is nonempty
and closed, and a minimizing sequence can be restricted to a bounded ball.
The zero row at i=0 gives <x0,v0>>=||v0||^2, while every polar point has
nonpositive pairing; therefore r(x0)>=||v0||^2>0 for a legal initializer.
The closest-minimum normalized score is Q(x0)=q(x0)/r(x0), in W=2R
normalization. All initializer potentials are represented because V^T is
invertible. The numerical implementation never forms an inverse Gram.

## Exact perspective and both directions

Variables b,zstar in R^(n+1) and tau>=0 define F=V^T b. Consider

    maximize O=2 F_n-2 tau c_n,
    F_j-F_i<=tau C_ij for EVERY i,j,
    F_i>=tau K_i, V^T zstar<=0, ||b-zstar||^2<=tau.

The last inequality is represented exactly by the ordinary SOC

    ||(2(b-zstar),tau-1)|| <= tau+1,

with the explicit tau>=0 constraint retained. Squaring both sides yields
4||b-zstar||^2<=4tau, and the nonnegative right side makes this reversible.

Given any legal x0, choose its actual closest polar point y and set
tau=1/r(x0), b=tau x0, zstar=tau y. Homogeneity of the polar cone and
of the normal inequalities makes this a feasible perspective point.
The cone is tight and its objective equals Q(x0). Thus the perspective
supremum is at least the supremum over every legal initializer.

Conversely, any exactly feasible point with tau>0 gives x0=b/tau and
y=zstar/tau in Kpolar. Dividing the original rows proves full legality.
Its selected radius Rsel=||x0-y||^2<=1/tau, while r(x0)<=Rsel. Its
positive terminal numerator is q=O/tau. Hence

    Q(x0)=q/r(x0) >= Qsel=q/Rsel >= O.

This proves equality of the two suprema when the legal set is nonempty;
it does not assert that an arbitrary feasible zstar is a closest point.
At an attained positive optimum, failure of either distance equality would
allow mapping the same initializer back using its closest point to produce
a strictly larger objective. This analytical observation is not a license
to declare floating output exactly optimal or its returned zstar closest.

At tau=0 the cone forces b=zstar. Zero rows force F>=0, while polar rows
force F<=0, so F=0; independence gives b=zstar=0 and O=0. The zero point
is always feasible. Therefore the program value is precisely the supremum
of {0} union all legal initializer scores, even if the legal set is empty.
The conventional supremum of an empty legal set is not this zero value.

For floating output, O, Qsel, and a claim about Q must stay separate.
Positive numerical residuals invalidate an exact feasibility assertion;
no returned ratio becomes a rigorous lower bound without repairing every
normal and polar defect by an independently authorized exact argument.

## Two exact calibrations, derived before execution

1. Empty word, v0=1: P=A=C=0, G=K=1 and c=1/2. Here F=b,
   b>=tau, zstar<=0 and (b-zstar)^2<=tau. Thus b>=0, tau>=b^2 and
   O=2b-tau<=2b-b^2<=1. At tau=b=1,zstar=0 every original row and
   cone hold and O=1. This is the exact optimum, also Q=1.
2. h=(1), v0=(1,0), v1=(1/2,1/2). The determinant of V is 1/2,
   so this is a full-rank two-dimensional fixture. Direct inner products give

       G=[[1,1/2],[1/2,1/2]], P0=0, P1=(1,0),
       A=[[0,0],[1,1/2]], C=[[0,-1/2],[1/2,0]],
       K=(1,1), c_1=3/4.

   Let p=V^T x0. The rows 0->1 and 1->0 respectively give
   p1-p0<=-1/2 and p0-p1<=1/2. Thus p1-p0=-1/2 exactly.
   Write p1=u, p0=u+1/2. The two zero rows are u+1/2>=1 and u>=1,
   which together are equivalent to u>=1. Diagonal ordered rows are zero.
   Solving these two inner-product equations directly, without an inverse
   Gram, gives every legal initializer and no others:

       x0=(u+1/2,u-1/2), u>=1.

   In particular x0=v0+(2u-1)v1, whose two coefficients are positive.
   For every z in Kpolar, <z,v0><=0 and <z,v1><=0, hence <x0,z><=0.
   The exact norm comparison

       ||x0-z||^2=||x0||^2+||z||^2-2<x0,z> >= ||x0||^2

   has equality at z=0; for any z!=0 its ||z||^2 term makes it strict.
   Consequently zero is the unique closest polar point for every legal x0,
   and the exact squared distance is 2u^2+1/2, strictly positive on u>=1.
   The exact doubled terminal gap is 2(u-c_1)=2u-3/2>=1/2>0.
   The score in the W=2R normalization therefore is

       Q(u)=(2u-3/2)/(2u^2+1/2), u>=1.

   Its derivative is

       Q'(u)=(-4u^2+6u+1)/(2u^2+1/2)^2.

   The numerator has roots u_-=(3-sqrt(13))/4<0 and
   u_+=(3+sqrt(13))/4>1. The denominator never vanishes. The derivative
   is positive on [1,u_+) and negative on (u_+,infinity), so u_+ is the
   unique maximum on the entire legal domain. The endpoint is Q(1)=1/5,
   and Q(u) tends to zero as u tends to infinity. At the positive root,
   4u_+^2-6u_+-1=0 gives

       Q(u_+)=1/(2u_+)=2/(3+sqrt(13))=(sqrt(13)-3)/2.

   To check the perspective directly, any tau>0 feasible point divides
   to this legal x0 and a polar y. Its cone imposes
   tau<=1/||x0-y||^2<=1/(2u^2+1/2), so its positive objective is at most
   Q(u). Conversely, at every u>=1 set tau=1/(2u^2+1/2), b=tau*x0,
   zstar=0. Every original row holds and the cone is tight, attaining Q(u).
   At tau=0 the general zero-scale argument gives objective zero, smaller
   than Q(u_+). The perspective optimum is exactly the displayed radical.

Both calibrations use the same matrix/prefix/constraint builder and one solve.
Their V,P,G,A,C,K,c values are independently compared with the explicit
fixtures. All discrepancies must be exactly zero in addition to the numerical
solver gates. The positive fixture exercises nonzero constants in both
directed rows. This symbolic derivation is an unverified research draft;
it is distinct from the forthcoming floating calibration and is not a new
Danus fact. The radical is evaluated numerically only inside its charged child.

## Frozen finite Gray construction

Depth j is 4 or 5; N=2^j, n=N-1. The physical enclosing parameter is
M=1536 in every production case. Define eps=1/(4M), beta=1-eps,
c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c, H_l=1+a c^l,
h_k=H_(nu2(k+1)). Let Gray(k)=k xor floor(k/2). The N rows of w_k
consist of characters chi_t(Gray(k)) with all nonzero t=1,...,N-1,
followed by the constant coordinate 1/sqrt(3). The j distinguished
characters t_l=N-2^l have squared amplitude 2^l/(N-1); every other
nonzero character has squared amplitude eta=1/[N^2(N-1-j)]. Thus
Tframe=4/3+1/N^2 and direct character summation gives

    B_kl=Tframe-2(k xor l)/(N-1)+2eta popcount(k xor l)
          -eta N+eta N 1_(k=l).

The implementation constructs characters first and independently exports
every raw signed discrepancy between their inner products and this kernel.
Walsh orthogonality with all positive amplitudes, including the vertical
row, gives independence; multiplying columns by positive sigma preserves it.
For each level let E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta(N/2-l-1),
Badj_l=Tframe-2E_l. Let rho_l be the positive root of
rho^2+(H_l-2)(Badj_l/Tframe)rho+1-H_l=0. The constant term is negative
since H_l>1, so exactly one root is positive. No numerical root is optimized.
For damping delta, r0=rho0(1-delta/N), r1=rho1(1-delta/N), other r_l=1.
All selected N exceed delta. Sigma is constructed chronologically from
sigma0=1 and sigma_(k+1)=sigma_k r_(nu2(k+1)), then v_k=sigma_k w_k.

Among integers 1,...,k, there are ceil(k/2) odd integers and
floor((k+2)/4) integers of valuation one (2,6,10,...). Hence, writing
x=r0,y=r1, the independent closed expression is
sigma_k=x^ceil(k/2)y^floor((k+2)/4). In particular sigma2=x*y.
Every signed chronological-minus-closed floating discrepancy is retained.
No use is made of the old erroneous floor((k+1)/4) count.

Each finite depth changes the character dictionary, eta, weights, and
radial law. These are not submatrices of the full 2^1536 construction.
D_j=1+2 sum h_k and D_j O are finite discovery diagnostics only.

## Frozen execution and reporting semantics

CASES.json fixes four jobs in order: the two calibrations, then
(N,delta)=(16,2),(32,2). The total limit is four jobs / 80 accounted seconds.
The stdlib broker precharges 20 seconds per attempted job and starts one
new process group via the existing public launcher. It waits at most 20
seconds measured from immediately before Popen, kills the whole group on
timeout, records every outcome, and never retries. Either calibration
failure retires all unlaunched slots. All imports, construction, one
CLARABEL solve, residuals, transcript extraction and exports occur inside
the timed child. No numerical setup or array reanalysis occurs in the broker.

Solver options are max_iter=120, max_threads=1, time_limit=10.0,
tol_gap_abs=tol_gap_rel=tol_feas=1e-9, warm_start=False. No fallback,
secondary projection, inverse Gram, graph algorithm or data reuse occurs.
Calibration passes require optimal status, absolute objective error<=1e-7,
all original scaled signed residuals<=1e-7, SOC and squared-cone signed
residuals<=1e-7, and complete exports. Neither calibration has a zero-tau gate.
The exact construction fixture discrepancies must be zero as an additional
builder check. Every residual is exported with its sign, including positive
defects; the tolerance classification never alters the values.

For extraction only, robust positivity means finite tau>1e-7 and
tau>100*abs(||b-zstar||^2-tau). This is a frozen numerical division guard,
not an exact positivity proof. When it passes, save x0=b/tau, y=zstar/tau,
all prescribed x_i=x0-P_i and v_i, all unscaled pair/zero normals, all

# Frozen output schema

Allocation 287b7a3644e5bda3; resume 5b362b0a381a2444.
All numerical contents below are produced inside the external 20-second child.

Each attempted case receives stdout.txt, stderr.txt, PRECHARGE.json and a broker
receipt.json with process identity, deadline, exit status, elapsed wall time,
calibration verdict, file-presence flags and byte hashes. A killed or failed
child may have partial exports; that is not a complete or passing case.

Complete child exports are:

- input_arrays.npz: V,h,P,G,A,C,K,cn,D, and for Gray all original characters,
  weights, W, Gray labels, original frame Gram, independent kernel and signed
  difference, chronological levels and radii, closed radii and signed
  differences, root/radial data. Fixture cases include every exact-builder
  comparison. No inverse Gram, dropped character, radius clipping or geometry
  optimization is used.
- program_data.npz: the canonical sparse conic constraint matrix components,
  shape, right side, objective vector and quadratic components if present.
- solver_data.json: versions, requested options, status, CVXPY solver statistics,
  all original constraint duals and primal variables b,zstar,tau,F. The one
  CLARABEL solve's available canonical x,s,z and primal/dual objective/residual
  data are exported through its existing solution accessor; accessor failure
  is explicitly flagged, never repaired by a second solve.
- arrays.npz: input arrays plus returned b,zstar,tau,F, signed scaled ordered,
  zero and polar residuals, squared-cone and SOC defects, tau defect, all
  available original and canonical dual/primal outputs. With robust positive
  tau only: x0, selected polar point, prescribed X and gradients, initializer
  potentials, all signed unscaled ordered/zero/polar defects, direct normal
  evaluations and discrepancies, GD recurrence defects, prescribed function
  values, terminal numerator, selected radius and selected-polar score.
- result.json: solver status, objective, construction statistics, each signed
  residual family's min/max/absolute maximum and positive count, tau/guard
  flags, extraction status, program objective and D_j times objective, separate
  selected-polar score and D_j times that score, and true-distance status.
  Calibrations add in-child expected objective, objective error, each gate and
  exact failure reasons. complete_exports is a file-presence check; broker
  additionally requires exit 0, no timeout and a parsable result.
- transcript.json: the existing F,b,zstar,tau values as direct JSON lists or
  scalar, and if extraction passes the existing x0, selected polar point,
  initializer potentials, numerator, selected squared radius and score.
  This repeats already calculated quantities; it performs no additional solve.
- SUMMARY.md: a text snapshot of the numerical result before the final export
  completeness gate. The final result.json and broker receipt govern that gate.
- ERROR.json is saved on an uncaught child exception when the deadline permits.

No numeric residual is clipped. The extraction guard is finite tau>1e-7 and
tau>100*abs(squared-cone defect). A selected polar point is never labeled the
closest one from numerical output. The true closest distance remains numerically
uncomputed, including when the symbolic calibration has a known exact formula.

The stdlib broker writes budget.json, registration.jsonl, PREFLIGHT_HASH_RECEIPT,
per-job receipts and CLOSED.json. Each of four slots is charged 20 seconds
before launch. Both calibration gates are mandatory; a failure retires every
unused slot. Source edits stop at FREEZE.json. Later closeout may read saved text
and hash bytes, but may not load numerical arrays or launch another calculation.

# Fresh finite all-initializer discovery with a positive two-vertex calibration

Explicit user continuation after the October8 summary restores bounded research authority. This is a NEW native gpt-6-astra/ultra invocation and NEW numerical grant. All old budgets remain CLOSED. Application Goal remains paused; do not alter it or claim an active Goal. Read host OPERATOR.md and original project PROBLEM.md.

Work only in D:/Danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/gray_full_perspective_positivecal_20261008_r2. Read public shared inputs, not worker private memory/conversations. Existing public solver runtime/launcher may be used, without installation or service changes.

## Scientific question and old boundary

For one finite fixed Gray body and prescribed itinerary, optimize EVERY initializer potential and the polar-distance perspective. This is a discovery probe for full original-row legality and normalized payoff, not a theorem about all dimensions/functions or all depths.

Read the complete public packet research/gray_full_perspective_snapshot_20261008, specifically DERIVATION.md,REPORT.md,CASES.json,child.py,broker.py and its existing freeze/closeout. Its negative-cycle calibration returned optimal_inaccurate; it ran NO Gray production. Do not replay that closed broker, reuse its credit, weaken its gates or modify its files.

The new batch changes the second calibration to a positive, full-rank two-vertex fixture with nonzero directed constants; it retains the vector perspective and the solver precision rules. Derive this calibration independently before freezing or running anything. If its exact value below does not survive your derivation, STOP without mathematical launch; report the defect.

## Exact new calibration proposed by the parent

Word h=(1). In R2 take v0=(1,0),v1=(1/2,1/2), with these as columns of V.
Then
G=[[1,1/2],[1/2,1/2]], A=[[0,0],[1,1/2]],
C=[[0,-1/2],[1/2,0]], K=(1,1), c_n=3/4.
Both directed rows force p1-p0=-1/2; zero rows give p1=u>=1. Thus every legal initializer is x0=(u+1/2,u-1/2).
Its positive-cone representation is x0=1*v0+(2u-1)*v1. Since this lies in the polar cone's polar, zero is its closest polar point: prove the orthogonality/norm comparison directly.
The exact normalized score is (2u-3/2)/(2u^2+1/2),u>=1. Its unique maximum is at u=(3+sqrt13)/4 and has value (sqrt13-3)/2. Show all domain, derivative and endpoint cases, and the perspective equivalence. The calibration is rank2 and exercises nonzero original ordered constants, unlike a repeated-gradient fixture.

## Frozen cases and resource grant

Exactly FOUR mathematical child slots, each EXTERNAL20seconds including every import, array build, solver, diagnostic, extraction and export. Total budget4jobs/80ACCOUNTEDseconds, each full20precharged BEFORE launch. Every failure, timeout, inaccurate return, calibration, auxiliary/extraction or replay counts. No automatic renewal, fallback, retry, fifth job, secondary projection/solve, or post-closure array analysis.

Case order:
1. Empty word,v0=1, exact optimum1, same calibration as the prior builder.
2. Positive two-vertex fixture above, exact optimum(sqrt13-3)/2.
3. Full original damping2 Gray construction at j=4,N=16,physical enclosing M=1536.
4. Same at j=5,N=32,physical enclosing M=1536.

The finite Gray dictionary uses j, not1536, while physical step parameters use enclosing M1536. They are finite analogues, not submatrices of the2^1536 body. Retain distinguished character labels N-2^l and ALL tiny mixed characters. Construct actual chronological radii by cumulative multiplication, and compare the corrected floor((k+2)/4) formula inside each charged child. No optimization over geometry/damping.

Use the existing CVXPY/CLARABEL runtime. ONE solve per case:
max_iter120,max_threads1,time_limit10,tol_gap_abs=tol_gap_rel=tol_feas=1e-9,warm_start=False.
Both calibration gates require status optimal, absolute objective error<=1e-7, every original scaled ordered/zero/polar residual<=1e-7, SOC and squared-cone defect<=1e-7, complete exports, and exactly zero fixture matrix-builder discrepancies. Expected radical value is calculated only INSIDE the charged child.
Stop before production and retire every unused slot if either calibration fails. Exit0 is distinct from calibration PASS. Do not divide a tiny tau; retain the prior frozen positivity guard tau>1e-7 and tau>100*abs(cone defect) if extracting.

Retain the same vector perspective:
P_i=sum_(k<i)h_kv_k,A_ij=<P_i,v_j>,G_ij=<v_i,v_j>,
C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+G_ij-G_ii,K_i=A_ii+G_ii,c_n=A_nn+G_nn/2;
max2F_n-2tau c_n,F=V^Tb,tau>=0,
F_j-F_i<=tau C_ij ALLi,j;F_i>=tau K_i;
V^Tzstar<=0;||b-zstar||^2<=tau.
No inverse Gram in the implementation. Save program value, selected-polar ratio and true-closest status separately. Do not call a selected point closest or a floating transcript exactly legal.

## Freeze, context provenance and execution

Prepare source, broker, derivation, cases and complete output schema BEFORE freeze. Freeze immutable copies of both HOST and CONTAINER OPERATOR/AGENTS/main-contract documents as separate provenance inputs; do not compare their live differing bytes as if identical. Every mathematical operation belongs inside a timed child, including imports/probes, matrix or eigenvalue checks, extraction and export. The broker is stdlib process/accounting/hash logic only. No mathematical setup outside a charged child and no inspection of saved numerical arrays after closure. Later work is text transcriptions, hashes, accounting and reports only.
Save all raw original frames/arrays,F,b,zstar,tau, objective, signed original scaled/unscaled residuals, primal/dual solver outputs and flags in the child. Positive defects remain positive, not clipped. Protect the entire child process group at its external deadline. Record actual complete child wall times and solver status.

## Output and limits

ONE native model invocation, ONE batch,4jobs/80accounted seconds max. ZERO formal submissions/fact_submit, additional models/agents/consults, installations, worker/service changes, private reads, truth-store writes, publication, finalization, revocation or shutdown. No closed grant reuse.
Deliver DERIVATION.md, frozen source/cases/manifest, actual raw exports, budget/registration/CLOSED receipts, REPORT.md and HANDOFF.md. Parent needs exact pass/fail reason, actual launched cases, tau/extraction status and every original-row defect. Numerical results are UNVERIFIED DISCOVERY only, original minimax target OPEN. Close the ledger and STOP with no automatic renewal.
