<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002516 -->
# Two global midpoint-potential laws fail for the original damping-two Gray itinerary

**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** This is a source-bound analytical proof draft from one
native invocation under allocation `4c874e7520d86e25` and resumption
`5b362b0a381a2444`. No verifier submission has occurred. The original
all-schedule exact Zhang--Jiang profile problem remains **OPEN**.

## 1. Result and exact scope

For the fixed damping-two itinerary in Section 2 and every integer
`m >= 1536`, define the **adjacent-midpoint potential** by

\[
 p^{\rm mid}_0=0,\qquad
 p^{\rm mid}_{k+1}-p^{\rm mid}_k
 =\frac{C_{k,k+1}-C_{k+1,k}}2.
 \tag{1.1}
\]

This differs from the previously rejected forward-tight potential. It
divides each adjacent two-cycle's positive slack equally between the two
orientations. The following original, nonlocal row nevertheless fails:

\[
 \boxed{C_{n0}-(p^{\rm mid}_0-p^{\rm mid}_n)
       <-\frac{a c^m}{4000}.}
 \tag{1.2}
\]

The second law is a **uniform physical-clock tilt** of (1.1):

\[
 p^{\lambda,\gamma}_i
 =p^{\rm mid}_i+\lambda s_i+\gamma,
 \qquad s_i=\sum_{k<i}h_k,
 \qquad \lambda,\gamma\in\mathbb R.
 \tag{1.3}
\]

No member of this second law satisfies every original ordered row. The
original row `(n,0)` would require `lambda > a/4000`, whereas the original
row `(0,1)` requires `lambda < 7/N`; these inequalities are incompatible.
Both statements include every tiny mixed character and use the inherited
chronological prefixes. No parameter is reset at a child.

This invocation therefore delivers the authorized **scoped obstruction**
outcome. It does not produce a negative directed cycle of the original
constraint graph. It does not exclude arbitrary potentials or all
initializers of this itinerary. The unrestricted global feasibility and
true-radius payoff questions remain open. No score is assigned to either
illegal law, and no comparison with `17/(16 D_m)` is made.

The source and TASK hashes checked during this invocation are:

| File | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- |
| `../shared_evidence/a37b81ea81b53910.md` | `abd330128e7f27d981712b19fca4fa0026ce2b87e91cb10b78b3ee2d49366356` |
| `TASK.md` | `bfcd0763aa8575b1e79e833f23febb1a594a6d178fd1a8662af120fac1f0f438` |

The full damping-two source was read, including its old failed law and its
pair relaxation. The complete all-initializer derivation
`../gray_full_perspective_snapshot_20261008/DERIVATION.md` was also read.
The corrected damping-four report was consulted for scope only; no
damping-four estimate is a premise below. The first attempted source
path omitted `research/` and returned `Cannot find path`; the corrected
source path above was then read successfully and hashed.

## 2. Frozen finite object and the legality interface

Fix one integer `m >= 1536` throughout. Write

\[
 N=2^m,\quad n=N-1,\quad \epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad
 \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
\]
\[
 H_\ell=1+a c^\ell,\qquad
 h_k=H_{\nu_2(k+1)}\quad(0\le k<n).
 \tag{2.1}
\]

Put `Kmix=N-1-m` and `eta=1/(N^2 Kmix)`. For the nonzero characters
`b` of `F_2^m`, the distinguished labels `b=N-2^ell` have squared
amplitude `2^ell/(N-1)`. Every remaining nonzero label has squared
amplitude `eta`. With a final constant coordinate `1/sqrt(3)`, set

\[
 w_k=((\omega_b\chi_b(\operatorname{Gray}(k)))_{b\ne0},1/\sqrt3),
 \quad T=\frac43+\frac1{N^2},
\]
\[
 B_{ij}=\langle w_i,w_j\rangle
 =T-\frac{2(i\mathbin{\rm xor}j)}{N-1}
   +2\eta\operatorname{pop}(i\mathbin{\rm xor}j)
   -\eta N+\eta N\,1_{i=j}.
 \tag{2.2}
\]

For an adjacent step of level `ell`, define

\[
 E_\ell=\frac{2^{\ell+1}-1}{N-1}
          +\eta(N/2-\ell-1),\qquad B_\ell^{\rm adj}=T-2E_\ell.
\]

Let `rho_ell` be the positive root of

\[
 \rho^2+(H_\ell-2)(B_\ell^{\rm adj}/T)\rho+1-H_\ell=0.
\]

Use exactly

\[
 x=\rho_0(1-2/N),\quad y=\rho_1(1-2/N),\quad
 R_0=x,\ R_1=y,\ R_\ell=1\ (\ell\ge2),
\]
\[
 \sigma_k=x^{\lceil k/2\rceil}y^{\lfloor(k+2)/4\rfloor},\qquad
 v_k=\sigma_k w_k.
 \tag{2.3}
\]

Thus `sigma_(k+1)=R_(nu_2(k+1)) sigma_k` and `sigma_0=1`.
In particular the `4q+2` radius is `xy theta^q`, with `theta=x^2 y`.
The wrong old `floor((k+1)/4)` count is not used.

All amplitudes are positive. Character orthogonality makes the complete
Walsh frame invertible; positive column scaling preserves independence.
Consequently `V=[v_0 ... v_n]` is invertible and `G=V^T V` is positive
definite. Define the original prefixes and constants

\[
 P_i=\sum_{k<i}h_kv_k,\quad A_{ij}=\langle P_i,v_j\rangle,
 \quad C_{ij}=A_{ij}-A_{ii}+G_{ij}-G_{ii},
\]
\[
 K_i=A_{ii}+G_{ii},\qquad c_n=A_{nn}+G_{nn}/2.
 \tag{2.4}
\]

For `D0=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}` and

\[
 \Phi(z)=\max_{v\in D_0}(\langle z,v\rangle-\|v\|^2/2),
\]

the projection normal at `z_i=z_0-P_i` is equivalent to

\[
 p_j-p_i\le C_{ij}\quad\hbox{for every ordered }i,j,
 \qquad p_i\ge K_i\quad\hbox{for every }i,
 \qquad p=V^Tz_0.
 \tag{2.5}
\]

Indeed, the normal against `v_j` is
`<z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>=p_j-p_i-C_ij`, and its normal against zero is
`K_i-p_i`. Checking all generating vertices checks their convex hull.
Every potential vector is represented by `z_0=VG^(-1)p`.

The exact minimum set is the polar cone `Kpolar={z:V^Tz<=0}`. For legal
potentials the normalized score, in `W=2R` normalization, is

\[
 Q(p)=\frac{2(p_n-c_n)}{r(p)},\qquad
 r(p)=\min_{d\ge p}d^TG^{-1}d.
 \tag{2.6}
\]

The terminal half-square is retained. Equation (2.6) follows by putting
`d=V^T(z_0-zstar)`; the polar condition is exactly `d>=p`, and the
squared norm is `d^T G^(-1)d`. Positive definiteness gives attainment.
It is not permissible to replace this radius by `||z_0||^2` without a
closest-point argument.

## 3. Exact interval identity for the first global law

Define the chronological scalar action

\[
 \mathcal E_i=\frac12\sum_{k<i}h_k(G_{kk}+G_{k,k+1}).
\]

The potential (1.1) has the closed finite form

\[
 p^{\rm mid}_i=A_{ii}+\frac12G_{ii}
                 -\frac12G_{00}-\mathcal E_i.
 \tag{3.1}
\]

To check this, put `d_k=v_(k+1)-v_k` and use

\[
 A_{k+1,k+1}-A_{kk}
 =\langle P_k,d_k\rangle+h_kG_{k,k+1}.
\]

The increment of (3.1) is
`<P_k,d_k>+(h_k/2)<v_k,d_k>+(G_(k+1,k+1)-G_kk)/2`, which is
exactly `(C_(k,k+1)-C_(k+1,k))/2`.

For any original indices `0 <= i < j <= n`, set

\[
 U_{ij}=\mathcal E_j-\mathcal E_i
    -\langle P_j-P_i,v_j\rangle-\frac12\|v_i-v_j\|^2,
\]
\[
 L_{ij}=\langle P_j-P_i,v_i\rangle
    -(\mathcal E_j-\mathcal E_i)-\frac12\|v_i-v_j\|^2.
 \tag{3.2}
\]

Direct cancellation, with no altered trajectory, gives

\[
 C_{ij}-(p^{\rm mid}_j-p^{\rm mid}_i)=U_{ij},\qquad
 C_{ji}-(p^{\rm mid}_i-p^{\rm mid}_j)=L_{ij}.
 \tag{3.3}
\]

Thus this law satisfies **every** original ordered row exactly when
`U_ij>=0` and `L_ij>=0` for every interval. This identity by itself is a
reformulation; the substantive result below is the uniform negative
bound on the particular original residual `L_0n`.

Every adjacent row does pass. Its two residuals both equal

\[
 M_k=\frac12(C_{k,k+1}+C_{k+1,k})
 =-\frac{T\sigma_k^2}{2}
 \left[R_\ell^2+(H_\ell-2)(B_\ell^{\rm adj}/T)R_\ell+1-H_\ell\right],
 \quad \ell=\nu_2(k+1).
 \tag{3.4}
\]

For `ell=0,1`, `0<R_ell<rho_ell`, and the other quadratic root is
negative, so (3.4) is positive. For `ell>=2`, the bracket is
`(2-H_ell)(1-Badj_ell/T)<0`. Here `H_ell>2` and `E_ell>0`.

Every zero row can independently be paid by the common shift

\[
 \gamma_\lambda=\max_{0\le i\le n}
       (K_i-p^{\rm mid}_i-\lambda s_i).
 \tag{3.5}
\]

The finite maximum exists. This pays the zero rows for either law but
does not change any ordered residual. Its effect on (2.6) is not zero:
both `p_n` and the constraints `d>=p` change with the shift.

## 4. Uniform radial estimates used in the finite-to-dyadic comparison

The following bounds use the actual root law (2.3):

\[
 \frac{12}{5}<c<\frac{121}{50},\quad
 \frac25<a<\frac5{12},\quad
 0<\frac{a}{c-2}<1,\quad
 \eta\le\frac2{N^3},\quad \frac43<T<\frac{27}{20}.
 \tag{4.1}
\]

For completeness, `7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12` and `epsilon<1/6000` imply the
stated `c,a,T` bounds. If `lambda0=1+sqrt(2)`, then

\[
 c(c-2)-\beta
 =(2\lambda_0^2-2\lambda_0+1)\epsilon+\lambda_0^2\epsilon^2>0,
\]

which gives `a/(c-2)<1`. Also `N-1-m>=N/2` for this range.

For `ell=0,1` put `b=a,beta`, respectively. Rearranging its quadratic
gives

\[
 (1-\rho_\ell)(\rho_\ell+b)
 =\frac{2(1-b)E_\ell\rho_\ell}{T}.
 \tag{4.2}
\]

The quadratic is negative at zero and positive at one; therefore
`0<rho_ell<1`. Its exact energies obey `NE_0<1+3/N` and `NE_1<4`.
Using `rho/(rho+b)<=1/(1+b)` in (4.2) yields

\[
 N(1-\rho_0)<\frac9{14}(1+3/N)<\frac{13}{20},\qquad
 N(1-\rho_1)<6\epsilon<\frac1{1000}.
\]

For the opposite bound, the first inequality gives `rho_0>999/1000`.
In (4.2), use `1-a>7/12`, `rho_0+a<17/12`, `NE_0>1`, and `T<27/20`
to obtain

\[
 N(1-\rho_0)>\frac{280}{459}\frac{999}{1000}>\frac{121}{200}.
\]

Consequently

\[
 \frac{13}{5}<N(1-x)<\frac{53}{20}<3,\qquad
 2<N(1-y)<\frac{2001}{1000}<3,\qquad 0<x<y<1.
 \tag{4.3}
\]

Let

\[
 \theta=x^2y,\qquad \zeta=-\frac N4\log\theta,\qquad
 s=e^{-\zeta/4}.
\]

Integrating `1/(1-u)` gives `u<=-log(1-u)<=u/(1-u)`. Applying this to
(4.3) gives

\[
 \frac95<\zeta<\frac{7301}{4000(1-3/N)}<\frac{11}{6},
 \qquad \frac{63}{100}<s<\frac{16}{25}.
 \tag{4.4}
\]

For the second bounds, the first four terms of `exp(9/20)` exceed
`25/16`. For `u=11/24`, bounding its Taylor tail from the quadratic
term by a geometric series gives

\[
 e^{11/24}<\frac{35}{24}+\frac{121}{976}
          =\frac{4633}{2928}<\frac{100}{63}.
\]

Write `u_k=(k+1)/N`. The exact ratio of `sigma_k` to `exp(-zeta u_k)`
in the four residue classes is

\[
 \theta^{-1/4},\qquad y^{-1/2},\qquad
 x^{-1/2}y^{1/4},\qquad 1.
\]

All their logarithms are nonnegative and less than `5/N`: use `x<y`
and `-log x,-log y<4/N`. Since `exp(v)<=1/(1-v)` for `0<=v<1`,

\[
 0\le\sigma_k-e^{-\zeta u_k}<\frac8N,
 \qquad 0\le\sigma_k^2-e^{-2\zeta u_k}<\frac{16}{N}.
 \tag{4.5}
\]

These bounds are uniform over every original index and preserve the
correct chronological count.

## 5. A global dyadic midpoint sum is strictly negative

This section proves a scalar inequality uniformly for the same `c,zeta`
in (4.1), (4.4). They remain fixed when a dyadic level changes.

For `r>=1`, put `p_r=2^(r-1)` and

\[
 F_r=\sum_{q=0}^{p_r-1}
 \left[e^{-\zeta u}\left(\frac43-2u\right)
       -e^{-2\zeta u}\left(\frac43-\frac1{p_r}\right)\right],
 \qquad u=\frac{q+1/2}{p_r}.
\]

The absolutely convergent series to be bounded is

\[
 \mathscr B=\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}c^{-r}F_r.
\]

Each summand inside `F_r` has absolute value below 3 and `c>2`, which
proves convergence. We claim

\[
 \boxed{\mathscr B<-\frac1{1000}.}
 \tag{5.1}
\]

The first two levels have exact expressions

\[
 F_1=\frac{s^2-s^4}{3}\le\frac1{12},\qquad
 F_2=\frac{5s-5s^2-s^3-5s^6}{6}<\frac{11}{100}.
 \tag{5.2}
\]

For the second inequality, substituting `s<16/25` only in the positive
term and `s>63/100` in all negative terms gives an upper bound strictly
below `11/100`. These are rational inequalities, not evaluated Gray
trajectories.

Let

\[
 I=\int_0^1e^{-2\zeta u}\,du,
 \qquad A=\int_0^1e^{-\zeta u}(4/3-2u)\,du-\frac43 I.
\]

Then

\[
 0<I<\frac5{18},\qquad A<-\frac3{50}.
 \tag{5.3}
\]

To prove the second bound, write `t=exp(-zeta)`. Direct integration gives

\[
 A=\frac2{\zeta^2}
   \left[t-1+\frac\zeta3(1+t+t^2)\right].
\]

The first seven Taylor terms of `exp(9/5)` have sum greater than 6, so
`t<1/6`. Therefore the expression in square brackets is strictly below

\[
 -\frac56+\frac{11}{18}\frac{43}{36}=-\frac{67}{648}.
\]

Since `zeta^2<(11/6)^2`, it follows that
`A<-67/1089<-3/50`. The first inequality of (5.3) follows immediately
from `I=(1-exp(-2 zeta))/(2 zeta)`.

For `r>=3`, write `p=p_r>=4`. The function

\[
 f(u)=e^{-\zeta u}(4/3-2u)
\]

is convex on `[0,1]`, because

\[
 f''(u)=e^{-\zeta u}[4\zeta+\zeta^2(4/3-2u)]>0.
\]

Integrating `f(v-t)+f(v+t)>=2f(v)` on each midpoint cell gives
`p^(-1) sum_q f((q+1/2)/p) <= integral f`.
For the other term, the finite geometric sum is exactly

\[
 \frac1p\sum_{q=0}^{p-1}e^{-2\zeta(q+1/2)/p}
 =I\,\frac{\zeta/p}{\sinh(\zeta/p)}.
\]

For `0<=z<sqrt(6)`, with the ratios defined by continuity at zero, the Taylor coefficients
`(2j+1)!>=6^j` imply `sinh(z)/z<=1/(1-z^2/6)`. Thus
`z/sinh(z)>=1-z^2/6`. Here `z=zeta/p<11/24`, so

\[
 \frac{F_r}{p}
 \le A+\frac Ip+
      \frac{I\zeta^2}{6p^2}\left(\frac43-\frac1p\right).
 \tag{5.4}
\]

Summing the three geometric tails starting at `r=3`, and dropping only
the negative part of the final parenthesis, gives

\[
 \mathscr B
 \le\frac{F_1}{c}+\frac{F_2}{c^2}
     +\frac{4A}{c^2(c-2)}
     +\frac{I}{c^2(c-1)}
     +\frac{I\zeta^2}{18c^2(c-1/2)}.
 \tag{5.5}
\]

Every term is controlled by rational bounds with ample separation:

\[
 \frac{F_1}{c}<\frac7{200},\qquad
 \frac{F_2}{c^2}<\frac1{50},\qquad
 \frac{I}{c^2(c-1)}<\frac7{200},\qquad
 \frac{I\zeta^2}{18c^2(c-1/2)}<\frac1{200}.
\]

For example the last upper bound follows by replacing `I,zeta,c` by
`5/18,11/6,12/5` in the appropriate directions. Also

\[
 c^2(c-2)<(121/50)^2(21/50)<\frac52,
\]

so the negative term in (5.5) is below
`-(3/50)(8/5)=-12/125`. Adding these five rational bounds gives exactly
`-1/1000`, with strict inequalities. This proves (5.1).

## 6. Return to the full original finite row, including all tiny characters

By (3.2), the original residual of interest is

\[
 L_{0n}
 =\sum_{k<n}h_k\left[
     \sigma_k B_{0k}
    -\frac{\sigma_k^2}{2}(T+R_\ell B_\ell^{\rm adj})\right]
     -\frac12\|v_0-v_n\|^2,
 \quad \ell=\nu_2(k+1).
 \tag{6.1}
\]

The kernel (2.2), including its diagonal and all mixed-character terms,
gives the uniform bound

\[
 \left|B_{0k}-\left(\frac43-2u_k\right)\right|<\frac3N.
 \tag{6.2}
\]

For `k=0`, the difference is `2/N+1/N^2`. For `k>0`, it is

\[
 \frac1{N^2}+\frac{2(N-k-1)}{N(N-1)}
             +\eta[2\operatorname{pop}(k)-N],
\]

whose absolute value is at most `2/N+3/N^2<3/N`.
The exact adjacent energy also satisfies

\[
 \left|(T-E_\ell)-\left(\frac43-\frac{2^{\ell+1}}N\right)\right|
 <\frac3N,
\]

because

\[
 E_\ell-\frac{2^{\ell+1}}N
 =\frac{2^{\ell+1}-N}{N(N-1)}+\eta(N/2-\ell-1).
\]

Use (4.5) and `|Badj_ell|<=T<3/2`. The first product in square
brackets in (6.1) differs from
`exp(-zeta u_k)(4/3-2u_k)` by less than `14/N`. For the second product,

\[
 \frac12(T+R_\ell B_\ell^{\rm adj})
 =(T-E_\ell)+\frac12(R_\ell-1)B_\ell^{\rm adj}.
\]

The first term, after multiplication by `sigma_k^2`, differs from
`exp(-2 zeta u_k)(4/3-2^(ell+1)/N)` by less than `25/N`. The second is
zero for `ell>=2`; for `ell=0,1` its absolute value is less than `3/N`
by (4.3). Hence the entire bracket differs from

\[
 e^{-\zeta u_k}(4/3-2u_k)
 -e^{-2\zeta u_k}\left(4/3-\frac{2^{\ell+1}}N\right)
 \tag{6.3}
\]

by less than `50/N`, uniformly in the original indices.

The physical sum has the exact closed expression

\[
 S_m:=\sum_{k<n}h_k
 =\frac{a}{c-2}c^m+
   \left(1-\frac{a}{c-2}\right)2^m-1<c^m.
 \tag{6.4}
\]

There are `2^(m-ell-1)` occurrences of level `ell`; finite geometric
sums give (6.4). Its last inequality uses (4.1).

Now split `h_k=1+a c^ell` inside the sum of (6.3). The contribution
of the constant part has absolute value less than `3N`. For the other
part put `r=m-ell`. Its actual points are exactly

\[
 u_k=\frac{2q+1}{2^r}=\frac{q+1/2}{p_r},
 \quad 0\le q<p_r,
\]

and its energy coefficient is `4/3-1/p_r`. Therefore that entire part
is precisely

\[
 a c^m\sum_{r=1}^{m}c^{-r}F_r.
\]

This is an identity for the inherited word, not a smaller Gray problem.
In particular `c,zeta,N` have never been reset at a dyadic level.
Since `|F_r|<3p_r`, the omitted infinite tail has absolute value at most

\[
 a c^m\sum_{r>m}3p_r c^{-r}
 =\frac{3aN}{c-2}<3N.
\]

Apply (5.1), include the finite kernel/radial errors, and drop the
nonpositive endpoint term of (6.1). This gives

\[
 L_{0n}<-\frac{a c^m}{1000}+6N+\frac{50c^m}{N}.
 \tag{6.5}
\]

Both remainders are smaller than fixed fractions of the displayed
negative term throughout the stated parameter range:

\[
 \frac{50}{N}<\frac a{2000},\qquad
 6N<\frac{a c^m}{4000}.
\]

For the first, use `N>=2^20` and `a>2/5`. For the second,
`(6/5)^4>2` and `c/2>6/5` give

\[
 \frac{a c^m}{N}
 >\frac25\left(\frac65\right)^m
 >\frac25\,2^{\lfloor m/4\rfloor}>24000,
\]

where `floor(m/4)>=384`. Substitution in (6.5) proves (1.2).
Every finite correction in this passage has its sign or absolute-value
bound stated. No asymptotic equivalence is used to decide the row.

## 7. No uniform physical-clock tilt can repair the law

Consider (1.3) with arbitrary real `lambda,gamma`. The first adjacent
forward residual is

\[
 C_{01}-(p^{\lambda,\gamma}_1-p^{\lambda,\gamma}_0)
 =M_0-\lambda h_0.
 \tag{7.1}
\]

By the exact two-cycle identity,

\[
 2M_0=h_0\langle v_0,v_0-v_1\rangle-\|v_0-v_1\|^2
       \le h_0(T-xB_0^{\rm adj}).
\]

Here `h_0<3/2`, `E_0<2/N`, and (4.3) imply

\[
 T-xB_0^{\rm adj}=T(1-x)+2xE_0<\frac9N,
 \qquad 0<M_0<\frac7N.
 \tag{7.2}
\]

Thus (7.1) being nonnegative requires
`lambda<=M_0/h_0<7/N` because `h_0>1`.
The original reverse row `(n,0)` has residual

\[
 L_{0n}+\lambda S_m.
\]

Its nonnegativity, (1.2), and `S_m<c^m` require

\[
 \lambda>\frac{a c^m}{4000S_m}>\frac a{4000}>\frac1{10000}.
 \tag{7.3}
\]

But `7/N<1/10000` in the present range. Equations (7.1)--(7.3)
contradict one another. This excludes every common shift as well,
because `gamma` cancels from both rows. The contradiction is about the
two-parameter potential law; the unknown arbitrary initializer does not
have to possess that law.

## 8. What a successful global correction must still do

There is a useful necessary condition on an arbitrary correction,
without proposing a third law. Given any putative legal potential `p`,
define

\[
 d_k=(p_{k+1}-p_k)
      -(p^{\rm mid}_{k+1}-p^{\rm mid}_k).
\]

The two original adjacent rows impose

\[
 -M_k\le d_k\le M_k.
 \tag{8.1}
\]

The original reverse row `(n,0)` and (1.2) impose

\[
 \sum_{k<n}d_k>\frac{a c^m}{4000}.
 \tag{8.2}
\]

All level-zero edges have `M_k<7 sigma_k^2/N<7/N`. The same argument
as (7.2) for level one uses `H_1<2`, `E_1<4/N`, and `1-y<3/N` to give
`M_k<13/N`. There are `N/2` and `N/4` such edges, respectively. Hence

\[
 \sum_{\nu_2(k+1)\le1}d_k<\frac{27}{4},
\]

and every legal correction must satisfy

\[
 \boxed{\sum_{\nu_2(k+1)\ge2}d_k
       >\frac{a c^m}{4000}-\frac{27}{4}.}
 \tag{8.3}
\]

On the higher levels the available adjacent margin is exactly
`M_k=sigma_k^2(H_ell-2)E_ell`. Thus a legal correction, if one exists,
has to allocate a substantial positive action change at the larger
dyadic jumps; a uniform correction per unit physical clock cannot do so.
Equation (8.3) is necessary, not sufficient, and is not a negative cycle.

The remaining all-interval requirement, using the exact `U,L` of (3.2), is

\[
 -L_{ij}\le\sum_{k=i}^{j-1}d_k\le U_{ij}
 \quad\text{for every }0\le i<j\le n.
 \tag{8.4}
\]

This is the smallest residual global inequality in this mechanism after
the two laws are removed. It includes (8.1), (8.2) and all original
nonlocal orientations. Constructing such corrections, or extracting a
genuine negative cycle from their incompatibility, remains **BLOCKED**.
Equation (8.4) is an exact transformed feasibility problem, not claimed
as a solution or a new feasibility theorem. This invocation does not
propose or test another correction law.

## 9. Constraint, radius, and evidence audit

| Claim or constraint | Status in this invocation |
| --- | --- |
| Complete Walsh geometry and all tiny characters | Retained in (2.2); finite error bounds include them |
| All chronological prefixes | Retained in (2.4), (3.1)--(3.3), (6.1) |
| Both adjacent orientations for the midpoint law | Positive residual `M_k`, all original edges |
| Zero rows | Can all be paid by (3.5); this does not repair pair defects |
| Original midpoint row `(n,0)` | Strictly fails by more than `a c^m/4000` |
| Uniform physical-clock tilts | All excluded by the two original rows `(0,1)` and `(n,0)` |
| Other midpoint nonlocal rows | Not claimed nonnegative or classified individually |
| Original negative directed cycle | None established |
| Full-itinerary existence with arbitrary potentials | Open |
| True closest radius | Exact interface (2.6) retained; no legal candidate to evaluate |
| Score or `17/16` threshold | No full legal score claimed |
| Original all-schedule minimax target | OPEN |

All sums in the report are finite unless an absolutely convergent
auxiliary series is explicitly declared. The only infinite tail is
returned to the actual finite word with a quantitative error bound.
There is no dimension limit and no passage from finite numerical data to
an asymptotic claim. The score convention remains `W=2R`.

If the full legal initializer set were empty, its conventional supremum
would not be zero; the zero-adjoined perspective supremum would be zero.
This report establishes neither emptiness nor nonemptiness of that set.
For both explicit laws the legal subset is empty, so no putative formal
terminal numerator divided by a chosen radius is called a GD score.

Verification depth is source-bound plus the displayed analytical
derivations and a self-audit of signs, indices, finite-tail direction,
root branches, and normalization. This is not independent formal

# Conditional closest-polar payoff and the frozen common-shift search

Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`; allocation `6ec6f7d4e5e2bb35`.
Supplied model/effort: `gpt-6-astra / ultra`; this is allocation metadata,
not independent backend metering. Written before the first mathematical
run. The exact interface below is an unverified analytical derivation;
the six jobs are numerical discovery. The original minimax Goal is OPEN.

## 1. Objects, realization, and the exact legality condition

Let n=N-1, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), v_i=sigma_i w_i with sigma_i>0,
||w_i||^2=Tframe, and <w_i,e_vertical>=1/R. Put

    D0=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}, C=cone{v_0,...,v_n},
    P_i=sum_(k<i) h_k v_k,
    Gram_ij=<v_i,v_j>, A_ij=<P_i,v_j>, F_i=<z0,v_i>.

The frozen Gray dictionary at depth j has N=2^j and EVERY nonzero
Walsh character. The j distinguished character weights are
2^k/(N-1), 0<=k<j; each of the remaining Kmix=N-1-j characters has
squared weight eta=1/(N^2 Kmix)>0. Including the constant vertical
character gives an invertible Walsh matrix, and positive row rescalings
by sigma_i preserve independence. Thus every vector (F_i+tau)_i has
an actual realization z0(tau). This fact does not require a numerical
inverse and no inverse is computed. For dependent calibration vertices,
realizability is checked directly instead of using this argument.

Consider the finite convex function

    Phi(z)=max_(g in D0) {<z,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Completion of squares gives the unique maximizer p(z)=projection_D0(z).
Its characterization is <z-p,w-p><=0 for all w in D0. Adding this
inequality for two projections yields
||p(z)-p(y)||^2<=<p(z)-p(y),z-y>, hence p is 1-Lipschitz. The two
maximizing affine expressions bracket the increment of Phi; the
remainder after <p(z),y-z> is bounded by ||y-z||^2. Therefore Phi is
differentiable with gradient p, convex, finite, and 1-smooth.

For z_i=z0(tau)-P_i, the nonzero-vertex normal is exactly

    <z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>
      = F_j-F_i-[A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii].          (1)

It is independent of tau. The zero-vertex normal is

    <z_i-v_i,-v_i>=A_ii+Gram_ii-F_i-tau.            (2)

Consequently, IF every ordered row (1) is nonpositive in exact
arithmetic, all vertices v_i are the actual gradients exactly when

    tau >= tau_min := max_i(A_ii+Gram_ii-F_i).       (3)

The terminal vertex i=n is included and there is no physical h_n.
Under that exact condition the prescribed GD recursion holds and

    f_n(tau)=Phi(z_n)=F_n+tau-A_nn-Gram_nn/2.        (4)

At every tau in (3), (4) is at least Gram_nn/2>0. The saved production
potentials have POSITIVE raw residuals in (1); the common shift leaves
them unchanged. Neither (3) nor a small residual certifies exact legality.

## 2. The full minimum cone and the closest-minimum score

The minimum of Phi is zero, attained precisely on

    C^polar={z:<z,w_i><=0 for all i}.

Indeed a polar point gives value zero, and if <z,v_i>>0, a sufficiently
small positive multiple of v_i in D0 gives a strictly positive value.
This is the full minimum set. It is a nonempty closed convex set (an
intersection of finitely many closed halfspaces), so a closest point
exists in finite-dimensional Euclidean space.

Write q_i(tau)=(F_i+tau)/sigma_i and M(tau)=max_i q_i(tau). Because
A_00=0, (3) gives F_0+tau>=Gram_00=sigma_0^2 Tframe, and therefore
M(tau)>=sigma_0 Tframe>0. The explicit point

    zstar(tau)=z0(tau)-R M(tau) e_vertical           (5)

satisfies <zstar,w_i>=q_i-M<=0 for every original vertex. It is a
selected minimizer whose distance is exactly R M. It is not asserted
to be the closest minimizer.

For any y in C^polar, choose i attaining M. Then

    M=<z0,w_i><=<z0-y,w_i>
      <=||z0-y|| sqrt(Tframe).

Combining this with (5) gives the CONDITIONAL geometry interface

    M/sqrt(Tframe) <= dist(z0,C^polar) <= R M.       (6)

The geometry itself requires realizability and M>0; identification of
f_n as the actual GD terminal objective additionally requires exact (1).
With that hypothesis, define the closest-minimum normalized score
Q(tau)=2 f_n(tau)/dist(z0(tau),C^polar)^2. Since f_n>0, (6) gives

    L(tau)=2 f_n(tau)/(R^2 M(tau)^2)
           <= Q(tau) <=
    U(tau)=2 Tframe f_n(tau)/M(tau)^2.               (7)

Scaling Phi by a Lipschitz constant Lsmooth leaves this score unchanged
when GD uses physical steps h_i/Lsmooth. Thus (7) has the project's
W=2R-normalization (the R in W=2R denotes the alternate performance
functional, whereas the geometric R here is sqrt(3)). The upper bound
in (7) concerns this fixed initializer family; it is not a worst-case
upper bound over functions for a schedule.

Every tiny character contributes to the frame norm. Explicitly,

    sum_distinguished b_t^2=(2^j-1)/(N-1)=1,
    sum_other b_t^2=(N-1-j)/(N^2(N-1-j))=1/N^2,
    Tframe=1+1/N^2+1/3=4/3+1/N^2,
    U(tau)/L(tau)=R^2 Tframe=4+3/N^2.               (8)

No tiny character was deleted in (8). Dictionary size N changes this
norm, the weights and the saved radial law. The four depths 6,8,9,10
are finite analogues with enclosing step parameter 1536, not restrictions
of N=2^1536 geometry and not asymptotic horizons for the original task.

## 3. Exact finite candidate set for the one-dimensional maximization

Set c0=F_n-A_nn-Gram_nn/2. On tau>=tau_min,

    M(tau)=max_i(a_i tau+b_i),
    a_i=1/sigma_i>0, b_i=F_i/sigma_i,
    f_n=tau+c0.

Sort distinct slopes increasingly. For an equal slope keep the largest
intercept, recording all indices when both slope and intercept coincide.
For the upper hull, a new steeper line meets the last retained line at
(b_last-b_new)/(a_new-a_last). Pop the last line while this intersection
is no later than its own activation point. Then append the new line with
that intersection as its start. This works because, after their single
crossing, the steeper line stays above the shallower line. Popping removes
an empty active interval; zero-length ties remain detectable from the full
original line list. No tolerance is used in these structural decisions.

Intersect each retained closed active interval with [tau_min,infinity).
On the interior where original line i is active, a positive constant
multiple of either objective is

    g_i(tau)=sigma_i^2 (tau+c0)/(tau+F_i)^2,
    g_i'(tau)=sigma_i^2 (F_i-tau-2c0)/(tau+F_i)^3.

Because tau+F_i=sigma_i M(tau)>0 there, the only prospective stationary
point is

    tau_i=F_i-2c0.                                 (9)

Retain tau_min, all ACTUAL upper-envelope breakpoints in the domain, and
each (9) lying in its own closed active interval. Include endpoint ties.
As tau tends to infinity, both objectives tend to zero, whereas their
value at tau_min is positive. The continuous objectives therefore attain
a maximum at this finite candidate set. Since U=R^2 Tframe L with a
positive constant, their maximizing shifts coincide exactly.

The numerical child constructs this hull with long-double arithmetic
from the unchanged saved binary64 values, then evaluates EVERY candidate
with M=max_i((tau+F_i)/sigma_i) over the FULL original line list. It never
substitutes a single hull line for this final maximum. Exact floating
ties and near ties are reported separately; the latter use only the
reporting threshold 256*eps64*max(1,|M|). The threshold does not change
the hull, candidates, residuals or objective. It is not a rigorous bound.
An interval-interior full-line audit and signed affine/direct evaluation
differences are saved. Extended precision reduces roundoff but supplies
no interval certificate. All raw defects remain visible.

## 4. Mandatory calibration targets derived before execution

Empty word: Gram=1,A=F=0,sigma=R=Tframe=1. Here tau_min=1,
f=tau-1/2, M=tau, and L=U=2(tau-1/2)/tau^2. Its derivative has
sign 1-tau on [1,infinity), so the maximum is exactly 1 at tau=1.

Three unit steps with identical gradient 1: N=4, Gram_ij=1,
A_ij=i, F_i=0, sigma_i=R=Tframe=1. A realizable initializer is
z0=tau. All nonzero rows are zero, tau_min=4, f_3=tau-7/2,
M=tau. The derivative of 2(tau-7/2)/tau^2 has sign 7-tau.
Its maximum on [4,infinity) is exactly 1/7 at tau=7. This dependent
fixture exercises coincident envelope lines; no Gray inverse is claimed.

Both use the same hull and score functions as production. Passing requires
the prescribed shift and tau_min, the target scores within the frozen
floating reporting tolerance, and nonpositive original/zero residuals.
Any calibration failure closes the allocation before production.

## 5. Frozen execution and evidence scope

Exactly six jobs: the two calibrations, then the four saved potential
lines. Each full 10-second charge is persisted BEFORE launch. The stdlib
broker starts a fresh process group via the public mathematical launcher
and enforces an external 10-second deadline, including imports, loading,
residuals, envelope construction, maximization, diagnostics and exports.
Launch errors, timeouts, exceptions and malformed output consume their slot.
There is no retry, replacement, post-freeze source edit or old credit reuse.

Production reads only its saved original_arrays.npz and graph_state.npz.
No graph relaxation, relative-potential change, residual repair, projection,
SDP, parameter search, benchmark DP or Gray reconstruction is performed.
Original binary64 residuals, reconstructed-constraint defects, shifted
normal roundoff, zero rows, candidate/full-line diagnostics and scores
are exported INSIDE the timed child. Administrative byte hashing and text
readback after closure do not recompute mathematics.

Exact legality, full-depth construction, closest-polar equality and useful
full-class payoff remain separate open questions. These finite numerical
surrogates close none of them. Formal calls, fact_submit, consultations,
subagents, worker/service control, truth-store edits, publication,
finalization, revocation, shutdown and Goal completion are all ZERO.

# Handoff: Gray common-shift payoff grant is closed

Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`; allocation `6ec6f7d4e5e2bb35`.
Supplied model/effort `gpt-6-astra / ultra`; ONE native invocation.
Original minimax Goal OPEN. This handoff is conditional analytical and
numerical research, not a verifier-accepted fact or legal GD witness.

Both exact-target payoff calibrations passed. All four prescribed saved
potential lines completed. The computation changed only the COMMON scalar
shift; no graph relaxation, relative-potential change, parameter search,
projection, residual repair or benchmark DP occurred.

## Exact conditional interface

For positive sigma_i, original v_i=sigma_i w_i, ||w_i||^2=Tframe,
vertical coordinate 1/R, saved F_i=<z0,v_i>, P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k,
A_ij=<P_i,v_j> and Gram_ij=<v_i,v_j>, define

    tau_min=max_i(A_ii+Gram_ii-F_i),
    f_n(tau)=F_n+tau-A_nn-Gram_nn/2,
    M(tau)=max_i((F_i+tau)/sigma_i).

The original nonzero normals F_j-F_i-C_ij, with
C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii, are unchanged by tau. IF they are all
exactly nonpositive, any tau>=tau_min makes the prescribed vertices the
actual gradients of Phi(z)=max_(g in conv{0,v_i}){<z,g>-||g||^2/2}.
The full minimum set is cone{v_i}^polar. The selected minimizer
zstar=z0(tau)-R M e_vertical gives distance R M, while Cauchy--Schwarz
on a maximizing frame row gives the lower distance M/sqrt(Tframe).
Thus the true closest-minimum score Q satisfies

    L=2f_n/(R^2 M^2) <= Q <= U=2 Tframe f_n/M^2.

This retains every tiny character: the distinguished squared weights sum
to 1 and all remaining weights sum to 1/N^2. Consequently
Tframe=4/3+1/N^2 and U/L=4+3/N^2. The selected minimizer is not claimed
closest. Full realization and proof details are in frozen `DERIVATION.md`.

For c0=F_n-A_nn-Gram_nn/2, the finite exact candidate rule is tau_min,
actual upper-envelope breakpoints, and stationary shifts F_i-2c0 lying
inside their own active intervals. All returned numerical candidates were
evaluated against the FULL original line list. Floating ties and near ties
are distinct; no reporting tolerance repairs any inequality.

## Results to carry forward

| Depth | Best tau | D_j max L | D_j max U | Numerical breakpoint pair | Original positive residual maximum |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| 6 | 25.523087175906153523 | 0.2481255719713320251 | 0.99268402048198679094 | [15,63] | 1.7208456881689926e-15 |
| 8 | 150.86963407113271378 | 0.2597180433982791591 | 1.038884062541636455 | [63,255] | 1.3822276656583199e-14 |
| 9 | 365.12724590724229257 | 0.26139465471823050458 | 1.0455816102973458261 | [127,511] | 2.8255175976710234e-14 |
| 10 | 882.55217876827418094 | 0.26220678927323691197 | 1.0488279072725891996 | [255,1023] | 5.684341886080802e-14 |

Here D_j=1+2sum h for the unchanged saved finite word. Best shifts lie at
numerical upper-envelope breakpoints. The terminal index alone is the
exact floating maximizer in each full-line evaluation; the indicated left
index is a near tie with a small retained positive gap. Both L and U choose
the same candidate. All selected zero rows are strictly negative numerically.

The empty-word fixture returned tau_min=1, best tau=1 and L=U=1. The
three-unit-step dependent fixture returned tau_min=4, best tau=7 and
L=U=0.14285714285714285714, matching the exact target 1/7. Both normalized
fixture scores D L=D U were 1, and every calibration check passed.

The selected-distance lower surrogates do not establish improvement over
1/D_j. For the three larger depths the closest-distance upper surrogates
remain above 1/D_j, so the actual closest-minimum payoff is unresolved.
All four numerical upper surrogates are below 17/(16D_j), but this is not
a rigorous obstruction or a class-wide worst-case upper bound. U concerns
only each fixed saved F's common-shift line; it is not an optimum over other
relative potentials, not an upper bound for the whole Gray geometry, and
not an exact-feasibility conclusion.

The production potentials still have strictly positive original residuals;
they are NOT exact feasible initializers. Common shifting does not change
that fact. The four depths 6,8,9,10 have enclosing word parameter 1536 but
dictionary N=2^depth. They are FINITE ANALOGUES whose damping and character
weights change with N, not restrictions of full N=2^1536 geometry and not

or original minimax resolution follows.

## Resources, artifacts, and stop

ONE batch, SIX jobs, SIXTY accounted seconds. Actual external child total

# Common-shift payoff: calibrated finite numerical discovery, grant closed

Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`; new allocation `6ec6f7d4e5e2bb35`.
Supplied model/effort: `gpt-6-astra / ultra` (allocation metadata, not an
independent backend-metering receipt). Original exact-profile minimax Goal:
**OPEN**. Analytical interface: **UNVERIFIED CONDITIONAL DERIVATION**.
Production results: **NUMERICAL UNVERIFIED**.

Both mandatory payoff calibrations passed. The four prescribed saved
potential lines completed without timeout or mathematical exception. Their
best selected-minimum lower surrogates have D_j L_max between the saved
values 0.2481255719713320251 and 0.26220678927323691197. The corresponding
closest-minimum upper surrogates have D_j U_max between 0.99268402048198679094
and 1.0488279072725891996. No useful improvement over the 1/D_j comparison
is established by these lower scores. The upper scores leave the closest
minimum undecided for depths 8, 9 and 10; their numerical values are above
1/D_j but below 17/(16D_j). At depth 6 even the numerical upper surrogate
is below 1/D_j. These are observations about four fixed saved lines under
an unmet exact-legality hypothesis, not exclusions of all constructions.

All four rechecks retained the original POSITIVE nonzero-row residuals.
The common shift cannot repair them. There is no exact feasible initializer,
certified terminal payoff, full-depth construction, or minimax conclusion.

## 1. Derived interface and what is conditional

For v_i=sigma_i w_i, ||w_i||^2=Tframe, <w_i,e_vertical>=1/R,
P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k, F_i=<z0,v_i>, A_ij=<P_i,v_j>, and
Gram_ij=<v_i,v_j>, all original nonzero normals are

    F_j-F_i-C_ij,
    C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii.

They are invariant under F -> F+tau. The zero normals become
A_ii+Gram_ii-F_i-tau. Hence

    tau_min=max_i(A_ii+Gram_ii-F_i),
    f_n(tau)=F_n+tau-A_nn-Gram_nn/2,
    M(tau)=max_i((F_i+tau)/sigma_i)>0,
    zstar(tau)=z0(tau)-R M(tau)e_vertical.

The full minimum set of Phi(z)=max_(g in conv{0,v_i})
{<z,g>-||g||^2/2} is cone{v_i}^polar. The point zstar lies in that
full set and has distance R M from z0. Any polar point y satisfies
M<=sqrt(Tframe)||z0-y||, by the maximizing original frame row.
Thus the closest distance d satisfies

    M/sqrt(Tframe) <= d <= R M.

If every original nonzero normal is EXACTLY nonpositive and tau>=tau_min,
the prescribed vertices are the actual GD gradients and f_n is the actual
terminal objective. Only then the true closest-minimum W-normalized score
Q=2f_n/d^2 satisfies

    L(tau)=2f_n/(R^2 M^2) <= Q(tau)
           <= U(tau)=2 Tframe f_n/M^2.

The selected zstar is a valid minimizer in that exact setting; no closest
point equality or projection computation is asserted. The upper score is
an upper bound only along the common-shift line of each particular saved F.
It is not an optimum over all relative potentials in the same Gray geometry,
not an upper bound for the entire Gray geometry, and not an upper bound for
the class-wide W_n(h). It supplies no exact-feasibility conclusion.

For dictionary depth j and N=2^j, the distinguished squared character
weights sum to 1, while all N-1-j remaining squared weights sum to 1/N^2.
Including the vertical 1/3 yields Tframe=4/3+1/N^2 and
U/L=R^2 Tframe=4+3/N^2. Every tiny character is retained. The complete
pre-run argument, smoothness/minimum proof and domain conditions are in
`DERIVATION.md`; none has been submitted to the verifier.

The numerical evaluation uses R^2=3 and the formula Tframe=4/3+1/N^2
in extended arithmetic. It records the saved binary64 R and Tframe defects
rather than changing the saved Gram, A, sigma, h or F. This does not assert
that floating arrays are an exact realization of the analytical model.

## 4. Frozen maximization and roundoff audit

The pre-run derivation writes c0=F_n-A_nn-Gram_nn/2. On an active line
M=(tau+F_i)/sigma_i, the only prospective stationary point is
tau=F_i-2c0. The code builds the increasing-slope upper hull, retaining
coincident original indices, and considers tau_min, all hull breakpoints
in the domain and stationary points in their own active intervals. The
objectives vanish at infinity and are positive at tau_min, so this is
the exact finite candidate rule for the analytical interface.

The installed runtime reported NumPy 2.5.3 and long-double nmant=63.
The production hulls had 7, 9, 10 and 11 retained lines respectively;
each domain produced two candidates. Both L and U selected candidate 1,
the numerical breakpoint, uniquely among the floating candidates. Every
candidate was evaluated against the FULL original M-line list, not merely
the hull's nominated line. Interior interval audits had maximum full-line
minus designated-line gap 0.0 for all four cases.

The maximum absolute affine-versus-direct full-line evaluation differences
across candidates were 5.898059818321144e-17, 2.393918396847994e-16,
8.881784197001252e-16 and 2.3314683517128287e-15. At the best candidate,
max(affine lines)-max(direct lines) was respectively
-5.898059818321144e-17, 6.938893903907228e-17,
-5.551115123125783e-17 and -3.3306690738754696e-16.
The threshold 256*eps64*max(1,|M|) is used only to list near ties; no
threshold affects hull decisions or repairs a residual. Neither extended
precision nor these diagnostics constitutes an interval certificate.

# One new Gray potential common-shift payoff allocation

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Original Goal OPEN; W=2R. Model gpt-6-astra, reasoning ultra. This is ONE newly authorized direct Codex invocation and ONE frozen batch, independent of the CLOSED old graph grants. Consult OFF, formal0. No renewal.

Read OPERATOR.md, relevant PROBLEM.md, and the full public reports research/gray_all_values_cycle_corrected_20261003/REPORT.md, HANDOFF.md and DERIVATION.md, and research/direct_gray_free_value_level_20261003/RESULT.md. No private worker memory. The saved graph batch returned numerical potentials with small POSITIVE residuals. They are not exact feasible initializers. Its four finite dictionary depths6,8,9,10, with enclosing word parameter M=1536, are FINITE ANALOGUES whose geometry changes with N; they are NOT restrictions of the full2^1536 geometry and NOT permitted asymptotic horizons.

Purpose: learn whether these particular saved potential lines have a useful normalized terminal payoff, before spending on exact construction. Do NOT rerun Bellman-Ford, change F, optimize relative values/steps/roots/Gram, solve a projection or SDP, run benchmark DP, or repair residuals. Only optimize a COMMON scalar shift of each saved F numerically, after deriving the interface exactly in text.

For the original vertices v_i=sigma_i*w_i, ||w_i||^2=Tframe and common vertical coordinate(w_i)_vertical=1/R, with R=sqrt(3), saved F_i=<z0,v_i>, prefix P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k*v_k, saved A_ij=<P_i,v_j>, Gram_ij=<v_i,v_j>, a shift tau changes all F_i to F_i+tau. Independently derive:
 tau_min=max_i(A_ii+Gram_ii-F_i),
 f_n(tau)=F_n+tau-A_nn-Gram_nn/2,
 M(tau)=max_i((F_i+tau)/sigma_i)>0,
 zstar=z0(tau)-R*M(tau)*e_vertical in the full polar minimum cone,
 M/sqrt(Tframe)<=dist(z0(tau),polar)<=R*M.
Consequently the normalized true closest-minimum score, IF the original nonzero rows are exactly legal, lies between
 L(tau)=2f_n/(R^2*M^2) and U(tau)=2*Tframe*f_n/M^2.
The upper/lower factor is R^2*Tframe; for the frozen dictionary derive it including every tiny character, rather than deleting them. These are conditional geometric inequalities, not a correction of the saved positive residuals. The selected zstar is a valid selected minimizer in the exact setting but is not claimed closest.

Freeze a one-dimensional upper-envelope maximizer before execution. On an active line i, M=(tau+F_i)/sigma_i, and the prospective stationary point is tau=F_i-2*(F_n-A_nn-Gram_nn/2). Retain tau_min, actual upper-envelope breakpoints in the domain, and stationary points in their active intervals, with ties explicit. Validate each returned objective against the FULL list of original M lines. All production calculations including loading arrays, original residual recheck, envelope construction, payoff maximization, diagnostics and exports must be inside that case's timed child. Do not load mathematical arrays outside the allocated children or after closing the grant.

Exactly SIX prescribed jobs, each externally process-group-limited to10 seconds, charged the FULL10 seconds before launch: total60 ACCOUNTED seconds. Actual elapsed seconds are separately recorded. A failed launch, exception, timeout, malformed output or interrupted case consumes its slot and10 seconds. No replacement, correction rerun, extra job or automatic renewal. If either calibration fails, stop before production and retire unused slots. Freeze code/cases/derivation and hashes before the first mathematical run; no post-freeze code edits.

Jobs1-2 are mandatory exact-target calibration instances of the SAME score/envelope code:
1. Empty word, one vertex: Gram=[1], A=[0], sigma=[1], F=[0], R=1,Tframe=1. tau_min=1, maximum L=U=1 attained at tau=1.
2. Three unit steps with identical queried/terminal gradient1: N=4, Gram all1, A_ij=i, sigma all1,F all0,R=1,Tframe=1,h=(1,1,1). tau_min=4, maximum L=U=1/7 attained at tau=7. This fixture is deliberately dependent; the common-shift score interface still applies and no Gray reconstruction is claimed.
Jobs3-6 read ONLY the saved original_arrays.npz and graph_state.npz for depths6,8,9,10 in research/gray_all_values_cycle_corrected_20261003/raw/. Preserve old files and hashes. Use saved h, Gram,A,sigma,F unchanged. Recheck all original ordered residuals, zero-row residual at the chosen shift, and raw roundoff in the full-line envelope evaluation. Do not declare a positive residual zero or exact feasibility. Report max L,max U, their D_j-scaled values for D_j=1+2sum h, tau_min, best tau, terminal f, M, active original indices, Tframe, factor and all raw defects. No benchmark profile DP or full-depth inference.

Use the existing public launcher research/run_shared_math_python.sh and installed runtime only. You may write a stdlib broker and one child in this new output directory, with all budget registration and process accounting public. No installation or alternate unbounded runtime. Account imports and mathematical output under each10-second external deadline. Broker itself does no mathematical array work. Administrative text reads, hashing, report writing and stdlib bookkeeping are allowed outside the mathematical child; they must not recompute mathematics after closure.

Write complete DERIVATION.md, REPORT.md and HANDOFF.md. The result must distinguish exact derived interface, numerical discovery, every failure, and open exact legality/full-depth/payoff gaps. Return after the six jobs or first calibration failure. ZERO formal calls/fact_submit, consult, subagent, worker/service control, truth-store edit, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or Goal completion. Only files under this new output directory may be modified. Old grants remain CLOSED, with no reused credit.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002528 -->
# max4 round64: inherited-parameter secondary-dual pattern report

**Completed numerical discovery; no new fact; original minimax OPEN.** All six
authorized jobs completed: calibration, four full secondary-dual solves, and
one independently coded H4 readback. All five solver statuses were `optimal`.
The canonical ledger is `closed_exhausted`:180 accounted seconds,
41.86519651800336 measured external child seconds, five solver invocations,
zero remaining slots. No correction, failed job, timeout, retry, or fallback.

The useful pattern is asymmetric. The right child's finite multipliers nearly
reproduce the previous inherited child's finite multipliers, while its optimum
attachments have a different scale, near0.6316. The left child changes
substantially, with large backward coefficients near dyadic endpoints. At H6
there are also small, clearly resolved forward cross-block coefficients.
These observations suggest a richer recursive law; they do not establish one.

## Exact object and frozen objective

All four words use the SAME enclosing M=6 and tau=1/4:

    lambda_s=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=23/24,
    c=(25/24)lambda_s, a=(23/24)/c,
    H0=(), H(j+1)=(Hj,1+a*c^j,Hj), j=0,...,5.

The discovery words are the actual children H3,H4,H5,H6 of that definition.
Their smaller-depth parameters are not recomputed with enclosing3,4,5.
All physical steps and every gradient coordinate, including the terminal
gradient, are retained. The terminal gradient supplies no additional update.

Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original W=2R normalization and interpolation
convention. Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For independent formal
vectors(x0,g0,...,gn), use x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t and ALL distinct ordered rows

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Both optimum directions and both terminal directions are included. If
Q=F f+A vec(G), the numerical model is

    minimize J=sum w_ij*lambda_ij,
    lambda_ij>=0, F^T lambda=2e_n,
    C=q_target E00+reshape(A^T lambda) PSD,
    q_target=101/[100(1+2sum h)].

Thus the full intended identity is
q_target||x0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=<C,G>, with every value coefficient
cancelled. This level was a feasibility target, not an assumed upper bound.
There is no rank, support, product-kernel, or incoming-terminal restriction.

The exact weights were published BEFORE job1 in plan9be7ee41a1245d03 and
`preregistration.md`. Let N=n+1=2^j. For distinct finite labels i,k, let
ell=bit_length(i XOR k), the depth of their smallest aligned dyadic block.
Use w_ik=2^(ell-1)/N and w_(star,i)=w_(i,star)=1. Terminal n=N-1 uses the
same finite rule in both directions. This ONE linear functional is unchanged
in all cases; there is no second optimization or adaptive regularization.

Conditioning uses K_cond=1+sum h, a scalar distinct from M=6, and
D_cond=diag(1,1/K_cond,...,1/K_cond). The solver constrains
C_scaled=K_cond D_cond C D_cond PSD. Multipliers and value flow are in original
units throughout. Original and scaled maps/matrices and the inverse congruence
are saved. The independent H4 readback checks the inversion explicitly.

## Calibration and feasibility defects

For h=(3/2), the preregistered nonzero reference multipliers are
lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=lambda_(1,0)=1. At q=101/400,

 q||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
   =||x0/2-g0-g1||^2+||x0||^2/400.

The exact reference objective is5/2. The charged model evaluation had zero
matrix-entry and flow discrepancy against this reference. The calibration
solver returned J=2.499999943615519 with secondary gap1.645353719936793e-8.
Every preregistered calibration check passed. The raw calibration still has
minimum multiplier-1.2612436727442586e-8 and minimum original eigenvalue
-9.956744939364884e-9. Passing numerical tolerances is not exact feasibility.

The following values are copied from the saved in-job summaries. J is the
secondary objective, NOT the original PEP objective2f_n.

| Child | n | q_target | J | Secondary gap | Iterations |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| H3 | 7 | 0.03782489677601333 | 2.853465898662487 | 3.2180014208904595e-9 | 16 |
| H4 | 15 | 0.015182158499335989 | 3.107557434785927 | 1.241881131264222e-8 | 20 |
| H5 | 31 | 0.006138546371301919 | 3.5082763702999706 | 3.408905424606701e-8 | 26 |
| H6 | 63 | 0.0024832359777307244 | 4.175331273969408 | 3.1075637174637905e-8 | 38 |

| Child | Min original multiplier | Min eigenvalue of original C | Max absolute flow defect | Max secondary stationarity defect |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| H3 | -4.467548844850404e-10 | -8.858543968733082e-10 | 2.999822612537173e-13 | 1.1264832400215141e-9 |
| H4 | -7.671545584423017e-10 | -5.321077301619408e-10 | 1.5510535713475214e-13 | 3.198401099524517e-9 |
| H5 | -1.0079188874254564e-9 | -6.393948206750424e-11 | 1.5407675135747922e-12 | 1.1020659940186306e-8 |
| H6 | -6.512987218271212e-10 | -5.566065549119014e-9 | 7.918260491734941e-11 | 1.106039526632685e-8 |

There are20,82,362,1381 negative raw multipliers respectively; all are retained.
No eigenvalue or multiplier was clipped. The nonnegativity constraints are in
the model, but none of these floating-point outputs is an exact sign/PSD
certificate. Full spectra and residual arrays are preserved, not only minima.

For the secondary conic dual, let y be the flow equality multiplier, s the
nonnegativity dual, and Z_original=K_cond D_cond Z_scaled D_cond. Its saved
stationarity is w+F y-A vec(Z_original)-s=0; its objective is
-2y_n-q_target Z_original[0,0]. These are the actual primal/dual diagnostics
for J. The nonzero right side w means this dual is not automatically an
original interpolation-feasible transcript or a legal adversarial function.

## Different hierarchy coefficients and the suggested relation

The charged diagnostics compared each parent's two induced children against
the previous SAVED inherited child. The left child includes finite sample
labels0,...,r, and the right child r+1,...,n, where r=(n-1)/2 is the physical
root bridge index. Each comparison also retains both optimum directions.
The scalar fit is a descriptive closed-form least-squares calculation, not
another solver call. The relative residual is normalized by the parent vector.

| Parent / child | Right finite factor | Right finite residual | Right optimum factor | Right optimum residual | Left finite residual |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| H4 / H3 | 1.0007690219603347 | 0.007239491890668989 | 0.6315969265208133 | 0.018536547699762426 | 0.6446437457246462 |
| H5 / H4 | 1.00030299279191 | 0.018844299469799174 | 0.6318115947637603 | 0.021986792708540426 | 0.7859783772146531 |
| H6 / H5 | 1.0002894706516186 | 0.0054076217631775685 | 0.6314755899700785 | 0.024142689287816408 | 0.8786389603659166 |

The right finite and optimum coefficients therefore need separate treatment.
A single scalar copy of a full child does not describe these optimizers.
Even separate scalars do not give exact identities: the displayed residuals
are much larger than numerical stationarity defects. The left finite block
is particularly far from a scalar copy.

The H6 root row illustrates real coefficient differences. All entries below
are original multipliers, with r=31 and the physical h values retained:

| Destination k | Physical level nu_2(k+1) | lambda_(31,k) | lambda_(31,k)/(h_31 h_k) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 30 | 0 | 7.416348190399652 | 0.13653774941895674 |
| 29 | 1 | 1.311857903575235 | 0.017032583903911765 |
| 27 | 2 | 0.24672903616397593 | 0.0018396847590614928 |
| 23 | 3 | 0.0476281112112982 | 0.0001715116516773268 |
| 0 | 0 | 1.0386633518121214e-8 | 1.9122181539960158e-10 |

Destinations30 and0 have the same physical level but very different weights.
Position within the hierarchy matters as well as step level. The earlier root
rows show the same concentration near successive dyadic endpoints: at H3,
lambda_(3,2)=0.45711281504070095 and lambda_(3,1)=0.09836338877232588; at H4,
lambda_(7,6)=1.1297980965705121, lambda_(7,5)=0.23467954457630183 and
lambda_(7,3)=0.003942262395092079; at H5, lambda_(15,14)=2.8653798695720165,
lambda_(15,13)=0.5449511942690242 and lambda_(15,11)=0.09483804561591222.
This is descriptive evidence for level- and position-dependent corrections,
not an exact support law. All smaller coefficients remain in the raw data.

Within the top dyadic split of finite samples, the backward cross-block sums
are0.7435105990164873,0.7283108670456813,0.7243282976299297,
0.7252981469443092 for H3 through H6. The forward blocks of H3,H4,H5 have
no entries above1e-7. H6 differs: its forward block has six entries above1e-7,
sum0.0021584484010527287 and maximum0.0015462200468230186. Thus deleting
all forward cross-block coefficients would change the selected H6 result
well beyond its residual scale. This is not an impossibility result for some
other certificate with different support.

The full saved singular spectra also argue against declaring a universal
one-channel correction. H5's backward cross-block leading singular values
are0.22003430918415515 and1.0969626725889478e-7, whereas H6's are
0.1829102833539127 and0.001228526081829379. The H6 forward block has leading
singular values0.0016618026040199432 and3.3281783246296826e-8. These are
threshold-dependent numerical observations; no exact rank is asserted.

The useful next analytical hypothesis is an asymmetric parent construction:
retain most of the right finite block, transport its optimum attachments
separately, and add position-dependent left and cross-block corrections.
The observed near-copy relation, optimum scale, and dyadic endpoint pattern
are clues to derive from the full identity, not premises. The data contradict
a faithful description by one scalar child copy or the previous two-parameter
product kernel; they neither exclude other duals nor prove a recursion.

Incoming terminal sums are1.2896450517851894e-9,7.309053742620733e-9,
3.061772810715e-8,4.16240364021452e-8. These rows were all present in the
model and saved without deletion. Their small numerical values are not exact
zeros. Support counts above1e-7 are28,72,162,416; at1e-9 they are37,153,553,1747.
The threshold sensitivity is another reason to retain the complete arrays.

## Independent original-cone readback

Slot6 imported no solver or solve module. It reconstructed every H4 Q matrix
by explicit outer products, accumulated all original finite value coefficients,
regenerated the SAME enclosing-M6 H4 and weights independently, and undid
the saved congruence. The saved results were:

* All272 ordered rows retained; min multiplier-7.671545584423017e-10.
* Max absolute original flow residual1.5510535713475214e-13.
* Original and scaled matrix differences from the saved solve:0 and0.
* Original minimum eigenvalue-5.321077301619408e-10, unchanged.
* Congruence inverse difference2.7755575615628914e-17.
* Word, target q, exact weight rule, and conditioning-vector differences:0.
* Secondary stationarity maximum3.198401099524517e-9; secondary gap
  1.241881131264222e-8, matching the saved solve.

Source H4 raw SHA256:
`d2ab9e06baa54a2c1398b3ddcde242eef2d47a5554538d4c199c018ceb7d11d0`.
This independently checks model/convention/export agreement. It does not
repair the negative multipliers or eigenvalues and is not formal verification.

# max4 round64: frozen inherited-M secondary-dual discovery

Status before all jobs: numerical discovery only; no theorem, formal call,
original PEP primal maximization, new benchmark computation, or installation.
Guidance970c491889a34251 and TASK.md allocate six whole-child slots of30s,
180 accounted seconds. Reserve the full slot before any mathematical import.
Every import/build/solve/check/pattern calculation/raw export is inside that
external cap. One solver call per solve job. Single threaded existing software.
All prior grants remain closed. Guard1311->1491 is reservation, not elapsed time.

## Exact words and normalization

For every discovery case, the enclosing parameter is M_enclosing=6 and
tau=1/4. Set lambda_s=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=23/24,
c=lambda_s*25/24, a=beta_word/c. Set H0=(),
H(j+1)=(Hj,1+a*c^j,Hj). Solve exactly the specified four children H3,H4,H5,H6.
Their n are7,15,31,63. Never replace their parameters by enclosing j.
Calibration alone uses h=(3/2), n=1. Arithmetic evaluations are binary64.

Use fact1252b8823bb5a290, the original W=2R normalization, L=1,
x_star=g_star=f_star=0, and the full formal basis(x0,g0,...,gn).
Physical x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t. There is no update using g_n.
For ALL distinct ordered i,j in {star,0,...,n},
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.
Write Q=F f+A vec(G), with row-major full symmetric Gram entries.
All original rows remain. Diagonal Q_ii=0 identities have no variable.

Fix q_target=101/[100(1+2sum h)] in each case. This is a feasibility target,
not a previously established upper bound. The sole optimization variables
are the original multipliers lambda_ij, all constrained nonnegative.
Require F^T lambda=2e_n and C=q_target E00+reshape(A^T lambda) PSD on
ALL n+2 original coordinates. The identity is
q_target||x0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=<C,G>.
No rank, pair support, terminal direction, product-form, or boundary restriction.

## ONE exact linear objective, unchanged across cases

Let N=n+1=2^j (j=1 for calibration). For distinct finite labels i,k in
{0,...,N-1}, define ell(i,k) as the unique positive integer such that
2^(ell-1)<=i XOR k<2^ell. Thus ell is the depth of their smallest aligned
dyadic block. Set

    w_(i,k)=2^(ell(i,k)-1)/N,
    w_(star,i)=w_(i,star)=1.

Minimize J=sum_(i!=k)w_(i,k)*lambda_(i,k). These positive rational weights
favor fine dyadic pairs. Terminal label n=N-1 uses the SAME finite-label
formula in both directions. The optimum rows always have weight1.
There is no other penalty, tie-break optimization, scan, or adaptive regularizer.
All integer weight numerators and the common denominator N are saved.

## Invertible conditioning and secondary dual diagnostics

Let K_cond=1+sum h, unrelated to M_enclosing. Define
D_cond=diag(1,1/K_cond,...,1/K_cond), C_scaled=K_cond D_cond C D_cond,
A_scaled=A diag(vec(K_cond D_cond outer D_cond)). Constrain C_scaled PSD.
Multipliers, objective, and value flow stay in ORIGINAL units.
No objective rescaling is used. Save C and C_scaled and undo the congruence
explicitly. If Z_scaled is the secondary PSD dual, y its equality dual,
and s its nonnegativity dual, save also Z_original=K_cond D_cond Z_scaled D_cond.
Stationarity is w+F y-A vec(Z_original)-s=0, with y free, s>=0 and Z>=0.
The secondary dual objective is -2*y_n-q_target*Z_original[0,0].
Save this objective, its gap to J, all residuals, and complementarity.
Z_original and y are NOT automatically an original PEP primal transcript:
the secondary stationarity has the nonzero right side w.

## Calibration certificate BEFORE execution

For h=(3/2), set lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=lambda_(1,0)=1,
all other original multipliers0. Every sign is nonnegative, finite flow is
(0,2), and at q=101/400 the full identity is

 (101/400)||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
   =||x0/2-g0-g1||^2+(1/400)||x0||^2.

The matrix is [[101/400,-1/2,-1/2],[-1/2,1,1],[-1/2,1,1]], PSD as the
displayed sum of squares. The preregistered objective of these multipliers
is5/2 because the two optimum weights are1 and w_(1,0)=1/2.
This is a feasible reference, not the asserted optimum of the secondary LP/SDP.
The charged calibration checks the model reference identity and flow to1e-12,
reference minimum eigenvalue>=-1e-12, then solver status optimal or
optimal_inaccurate, complete outputs, min lambda>=-1e-7, original flow<=1e-7,
min eigenvalue C>=-1e-7, scaled PSD>=-1e-7, J<=5/2+1e-6,
absolute secondary gap<=1e-6, and stationarity<=1e-6.
A failed calibration ends discovery unless the single correction is explicitly
used within the same six-slot allocation. No automatic retry is implemented.

## Frozen jobs and diagnostics

Normal order: calibration,H3,H4,H5,H6,independent_H4_readback.
Exactly one CLARABEL invocation per solve with max_iter120,max_threads1,
time_limit18,tol_gap_abs1e-8,tol_gap_rel1e-8,tol_feas1e-8. External whole-child
30s cap remains controlling. Timeout/error consumes30 accounted seconds.
The sixth slot is solver-free independent reconstruction of saved H4, unless
the sole necessary correction uses it. Failed H4 retires the readback slot.
No seventh job. No post-closure mathematical computation.

Save all original/scaled coefficient maps, positions, pair labels, weights,
original multipliers, full matrices and spectra, sign/flow/PSD residuals,
secondary primal/dual/KKT diagnostics, settings, actual precision, iterations,
counts, and nested times. Do not clip eigenvalues, threshold stored raw arrays,
or discard small multipliers. Structural thresholds1e-7 and1e-9 are descriptive.

Within each charged solve, summarize every aligned dyadic block's two directed
cross-half multiplier matrices: sums, extrema, thresholded support, full singular
values. Preserve all bridge rows and terminal/optimum rows. Report queried-prefix
lambda_ik/(h_i h_k) by direction and root destination level, with full root lists.
At H4,H5,H6 compare each induced child including optimum attachments to the
previous SAVED inherited child: best scalar least-squares factor and residual,
plus finite-only and optimum-only comparisons. These are diagnostics of the
chosen numerical optimizer, not support restrictions or additional optimizations.
Save both root directed cross-block singular spectra and all cone eigenvalues.

The independent H4 program imports no solver or solve module and uses explicit
outer products and flow accumulation from the saved original ordered labels.
It regenerates H4 under M_enclosing6, reconstructs q, all coefficients, signs,
flow, original PSD and conditioning inversion, and checks secondary stationarity
using direct quadratic forms. Preserve every residual; agreement is not a proof.

## Scope and provenance

ad24079f80cd6a3a is old standalone-m primal discovery; no old call is reused.
dd4045b39aa0c257 is an unverified scoped product-kernel obstruction, not an
impossibility premise. The public old solve_case.py was read for its original
Q coefficient convention; its runner is never invoked and its ledger unchanged.
Only the shared existing Python launcher supplies numerical packages.
The accepted fact supplies the model. Matlas returned two inapplicable lemmas;
web found the known silver-schedule paper2309.16530, whose different schedule
is not imported as a theorem about these inherited words. No new literature
premise, external_refs entry, benchmark comparison, or formal claim is made.

All new artifacts are under the assigned absolute public directory, except
updates to the specifically assigned canonical ledger and append-only existing
worker local memory. Hash the frozen sources and this document before job1.
Close and retire unused credit at round end. No automatic renewal.

# max2 round59 handoff

Status: one allocated analytical round complete; all new mathematics
UNVERIFIED. Original exact-profile Goal OPEN. No formal gate or further
round was authorized by this task.

Assignment: finite realizable perturbation of the exact same-word Huber
transcript on tau1/4. Completed baseline58, assigned completed ceiling59,
attempted ceiling60. Originating and latest-read guidance7b221a98307db859.

Complete joint evidence: proof_attempt6d0784199ba9ce6f, public file
`elapsed_clock_perturbation.md`, SHA256
`58bfdea59c2faec59068a5d62f38382bdecad2e37e546e1b06353b1679d482ea`.
Plan80f6f693d6dac1ed; counterexamplebd6c4bd06ca32f1a;
scoped dead_end38ea9b001fdf332e. No fact_id was created.

## What was actually constructed

For a positive physical word with S=sum h>=2, use the complete original
clocks P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t P_t. In R^2 set x0=e0 and

    g_i=sigma[(1-2epsilon P_i/S)e0+sqrt(epsilon)(P_i/S)e1],
    x_i=e0-P_i sigma e0-T_i(sigma/S)(-2epsilon e0+sqrt(epsilon)e1).

All0<epsilon<=1/16 are allowed. Put eta=T_n/S, alpha=1-2epsilon,
M=1+2S, J=1+2eta+4alpha(S-eta), D=M alpha^2+epsilon J,
sigma=alpha/D. One shared value array has terminal
f_n=<g_n,x_n>-||g_n||^2/2 and exact trapezoidal decrements.

The full Gram is explicit and PSD. The projected scalar derivative has
slope1/(d-P_i), d=2S/(1+4epsilon)>S+1; this pays every finite directed
interpolation row. Outgoing optimum heights decrease to zero at n;
all opposite optimum rows retain a uniform strict margin. Only after
these checks, accepted1252b8823bb5a290 constructs one static interpolant.
The strict opposite-optimum rows imply that this function has unique
minimum0 and true initial radius1. Its smoothness1 is sharp.

## Quantitative outcome and precise failure

The ORIGINAL terminal optimum row is an exact upper bound over every
common scalar scale and every reselected shared value array. The displayed
legal construction attains it:

    V_epsilon=alpha^2/(M alpha^2+epsilon J)<H_base=1/M.

At the fixed amplitude epsilon=1/16,

    (7/8)H_base<=V_epsilon<=(49/54)H_base.

The perturbation therefore remains at the benchmark scale but loses a
fixed fraction. Accepted5fd396eba75651e7 supplies the independent same-word
comparison: for all m>=1536 on the unchanged tau1/4 hierarchy,

    B_(2^m-1)V_epsilon<(49/54)(237/250)<1.

The failure survives arbitrary scalar retuning in this specified class.
If g_i=sigma[(1-delta P_i/S)e0+sqrt(nu)(P_i/S)e1], with0<delta<1,nu>0,
then, writing A=1-delta, the terminal row gives

    2f_n<=A^2/[M A^2+2delta A(S-eta)+nu(1+2eta)]<1/M.

This is a necessary bound, not an assertion that every retuning is legal.
Omitting the scalar correction altogether fails the first original
pair: Q01+Q10=nu sigma^2 h0^2/S^2>0, independently of all values.

## Remaining interface and resource handoff

No general Huber optimality, no unrestricted W upper bound, no finite
Huber-beating example, and no original all-word lower theorem was obtained.
The limitation is affine dependence on elapsed mass, initial-gradient
alignment with the unit radius, and a positive terminal longitudinal
component. Nonlinear temporal kernels, different initial correlations,
and terminal reversals are outside the obstruction. This is a useful
finite legality/payment calibration for a separately assigned future
kernel, not authorization for another mechanism or round.

All mathematical programs, numerical evaluations, arithmetic replays,
executed recurrences, solvers, benchmark DP, verifier/fact_submit calls,
installations, consults, and subagents: ZERO. Mechanism repairs: ZERO.
No old computation grant was reopened. Only administrative reads,
literature retrieval, symbolic reasoning and text/integrity persistence
were performed. The original task and latest guidance still agree.
The post-return outer-loop counter has not been observed.

The proof was read back in full. Broad initial recall output was truncated;
all required failure evidence and exact relevant scopes were recovered in
bounded reads before the policy was selected. The exact local primary
paper1502.05666 was read for terminology, and no external theorem was
imported. New correctness authority remains absent because formal
verification was explicitly outside this round's allocation.
